\bm{u}:[-1,1]^{d}\times\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{2}
, pressure
p:[-1,1]^{d}\times\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and temperature
\varphi:[-1,1]^{d}\times\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
of a fluid such that

\displaystyle-{\mathbf{div}}(2a(\bm{x})\varpi(\varphi(\bm{x}))\bm{e}(\bm{u}(\bm{x})))+(\bm{u}(\bm{x})\cdot\nabla)\bm{u}(\bm{x})+\nabla p(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=\varphi(\bm{x})\bm{g}\quad\text{ in }\Omega,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{div}}(\bm{u}(\bm{x}))

\displaystyle=0\quad\text{ in }\Omega,

(B.16)

\displaystyle-{\mathrm{div}}(\mathbb{K}(\bm{x})\nabla\varphi(\bm{x}))+\bm{u}(\bm{x})\cdot\nabla\varphi(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=0\quad\text{ in }\Omega,

\displaystyle\bm{u}

\displaystyle=\bm{u}_{D}\quad\text{ on }\partial\Omega,

\displaystyle\varphi

\displaystyle=\varphi_{D}\quad\text{ on }\partial\Omega,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}p(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=0.

Here
\bm{g}=(0,0,-1)^{\top}
is a gravitational force and
\mathbb{K}(\bm{x})=\mathbb{K}(\cdot;\bm{x})\in\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)
, where
\mathbb{K}:[-1,1]^{d}\times\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}
is a parametric uniformly-positive tensor describing the thermal conductivity of the fluid. It is given explicitly by

\mathbb{K}(\bm{z},\bm{x})=\left(1.89+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}x_{j}\frac{\sin(\pi z_{3}j)}{j^{9/5}}\right)\begin{bmatrix}\exp(-z_{1})&0&0\\
0&\exp(-z_{2})&0\\
0&0&\exp(-z_{3})\end{bmatrix},\ \forall\bm{z}\in\Omega,

(B.17)

for
\bm{x}\in[-1,1]^{d}
. The term
\varpi:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
is a temperature-dependent viscosity given by
\varpi(\varphi)=0.1+\exp(-\varphi)
and the term
a(\bm{x})=a(\cdot;\bm{x})\in\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)
, where
a:[-1,1]^{d}\times\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is a parametric variable affecting the viscosity of the fluid.
As in the previous example
\bm{e}(\bm{u})
is the symmetric part of
\nabla\bm{u}
. Note that in this case, we have
(a(\bm{x}),\mathbb{K}(\bm{x}))\in\mathcal{X}:=\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)\times\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)
.

B.5.1
Fully mixed variational formulation

The complete derivation of a fully-mixed variational formulation for the non-parametric Boussinesq equation in Banach spaces can be found in
[
20
, Sec. 3.1]
. To make the presentation simpler we rewrite it for the parametric case. Given
\bm{x}\in[-1,1]^{d}
, find
(\bm{u},\bm{t},\bm{\sigma},\varphi,\tilde{\bm{t}},\tilde{\bm{\sigma}})(\bm{x})\in\mathbf{L}^{4}(\Omega)\times\mathbb{L}_{\mathrm{tr}}^{2}(\Omega)\times{\mathbb{H}_{0}(\mathbf{div}_{4/3};\Omega)}\times{\mathrm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\times\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)\times{{\mathbf{H}}(\mathrm{div}_{4/3};\Omega)}
such that

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}\bm{v}\cdot{\mathbf{div}}(\bm{\sigma}(\bm{x}))\,+\,\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\bm{t}(\bm{x})\bm{u}(\bm{x})\cdot\bm{v}\,-\,\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\varphi(\bm{x})\bm{g}\cdot\bm{v}&=&0&\\[8.61108pt]
\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}2a(\bm{x})\varpi(\varphi(\bm{x}))\bm{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}(\bm{x}):\bm{s}\,-\,\dfrac{1}{2}\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(\bm{u}\otimes\bm{u})(\bm{x}):\bm{s}&=&\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\bm{\sigma}(\bm{x}):\bm{s}&\\[8.61108pt]
\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\bm{\tau}:\bm{t}(\bm{x})\,+\,\int_{\Omega}\bm{u}(\bm{x})\cdot{\mathbf{div}}(\bm{\tau})&=&\langle\bm{\tau}\bm{\nu},\bm{u}_{D}\rangle_{\Gamma}&\\[8.61108pt]
\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}\psi\,{\mathrm{div}}(\tilde{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{x}))\,+\,\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\psi(\bm{x})\bm{u}(\bm{x})\cdot\tilde{\bm{t}}&=&0&\\[8.61108pt]
\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mathbb{K}(\bm{x})\tilde{\bm{t}}(\bm{x})\cdot\tilde{\bm{s}}-\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\varphi(\bm{x})\bm{u}(\bm{x})\cdot\tilde{\bm{s}}&=&\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\tilde{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{x})\cdot\tilde{\bm{s}}&\\[8.61108pt]
\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\tilde{\bm{\tau}}\cdot\tilde{\bm{t}}(\bm{x})\,+\,\int_{\Omega}\varphi(\bm{x})\,{\mathrm{div}}(\tilde{\bm{\tau}})&=&\langle\tilde{\bm{\tau}}\cdot\bm{\nu},\varphi_{D}\rangle_{\Gamma}&\\[8.61108pt]
\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mathrm{tr}(2\bm{\sigma}+\bm{u}\otimes\bm{u})(\bm{x})&=&0,&\\[8.61108pt]
\end{array}

(B.18)

for all
(\bm{v},\bm{s},\bm{\tau},\psi,\tilde{\bm{\sigma}},\tilde{\bm{\tau}})\in\mathbf{L}^{4}(\Omega)\times\mathbb{L}_{\mathrm{tr}}^{2}(\Omega)\times{\mathbb{H}_{0}(\mathbf{div}_{4/3};\Omega)}\times{\mathrm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\times\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)\times{{\mathbf{H}}(\mathrm{div}_{4/3};\Omega)}
.
Here
p\in\mathrm{L}_{0}^{2}(\Omega)
can be recovered as

p=-\dfrac{1}{6}\mathrm{tr}(2\sigma+2c\mathbb{I}+\bm{u}\otimes\bm{u}),\quad\text{where }c=-\dfrac{1}{6|\Omega|}\int_{\Omega}\mathrm{tr}(\bm{u}\otimes\bm{u}).

(B.19)

As in the previous example, we omitted the term
\bm{x}
from this equation for simplicity.
For further details on this formulation we refer to
[
20
]
and references within.

Given
\bm{x}\in[-1,1]^{d}
, we approximate the solution
(\bm{u},p,\varphi)(\bm{x})\in(\mathbf{L}^{4}(\Omega)\times\mathrm{L}_{0}^{2}(\Omega)\times{\mathrm{L}^{4}(\Omega)})
of (
B.18
) by using DNNs and study the approximation capabilities as we increase the number of training samples
m
. As in the previous example (see Remark
B.4.1
), we do not aim to approximate the other variables
(\bm{t},\bm{\sigma},\tilde{\bm{t}},\tilde{\bm{\sigma}})(\bm{x})\in\mathbb{L}_{\mathrm{tr}}^{2}(\Omega)\times{\mathbb{H}_{0}(\mathbf{div}_{4/3};\Omega)}\times\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)\times{{\mathbf{H}}(\mathrm{div}_{4/3};\Omega)}
.

In our experiments, we consider the unit cube
\Omega=(0,1)^{3}
as the domain in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. We consider a nonzero boundary condition
u_{D}=(1,1,0)
on the bottom face of the cube
\partial\Omega_{\mathrm{bottom}}=(0,1)\times(0,1)\times\{0\}
, and zero on the other faces. We set
\varphi_{D}=\exp(4(-(z_{1}-0.5)^{2}-(z_{2}-0.5)^{2}))
on
\partial\Omega_{\mathrm{bottom}}
and zero otherwise. For simplicity, we consider the same parametric coefficients
a_{1,d}
and
a_{2,d}
given by (
B.1
) and (
B.2
), respectively. See Fig.
8
for an example of the solution
(\bm{u},p,\varphi)(\bm{x})
for a given
\bm{x}\in[-1,1]^{4}
.

Figure 8:
The solution
(\bm{u},\varphi,p)(\bm{x})
to the parametric Boussinesq problem in (
B.18
) for a given parameter
\bm{x}=(1,0,0,0)^{\top}
with affine coefficient
a_{1,d}
and
d=4
, using a total of
18,480
DoF for
\bm{u}
and
528
DoF for both
\varphi
and
p
. The top row shows the solution given by the FEM solver and the bottom row shows the
4\times 40
ELU–DNN approximation after
60,000
epochs of training with
m=500
sample points. The left plots show streamlines of the vector field
\bm{u}
and their directions indicated with coloured arrows. The middle plots visualize the temperature distribution inside the cube using coloured spheres, with the hottest region at the centre of the cube. The right plots illustrate the points of highest pressure
p
.

To conclude this section, we provide a comparison of the performance of the DNN architectures in approximating the velocity field
\bm{u}
and pressure
p
for the Boussinesq PDE. This complements Fig.
3
, which showed results for the temperature
\varphi
. As with
\varphi
, the convergence rate for
p
agrees roughly with the rate
m^{-1}
, and does not appear to deteriorate with the parametric dimension
d
. On the other hand, the convergence rate for the velocity field
\bm{u}
is somewhat slower.

Figure 9:
The same as Fig.
3
, except showing results for the velocity field
\bm{u}
, where
\mathcal{Y}=\mathrm{L}^{4}(\Omega)
, and pressure
p
, where
\mathcal{Y}=\mathrm{L}^{2}_{0}(\Omega)
.

Appendix C
Overview of the proofs

In this section, we first introduce additional notation that is needed for the proofs of the main results. We then give a brief overview of the proofs.

C.1
Additional notation

C.1.1
Lipschitz constants

Let
(\mathcal{X},{\|\cdot\|}_{\mathcal{X}})
and
(\mathcal{Y},{\|\cdot\|}_{\mathcal{Y}})
be Banach spaces,
G:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
and
\mathcal{B}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
. We define the Lipschitz constant as
L=\mathrm{Lip}(G;\mathcal{B},\mathcal{Y})
as the smallest constant
L\geq 0
such that

{\|G(X^{\prime})-G(X)\|}_{\mathcal{Y}}\leq L{\|X^{\prime}-X\|}_{\mathcal{X}},\quad\forall X,X^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}.

C.1.2
Sequence spaces

We require some notation for sequences. Let
(\mathcal{Z},{\|\cdot\|}_{\mathcal{Z}})
be a Banach space,
d=\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
and write
\bm{\nu}=(\nu_{k})^{d}_{k=1}
for an arbitrary multi-index in
\mathbb{N}^{d}_{0}
. If
\Lambda\subseteq\mathbb{N}^{d}_{0}
is a finite or countable set of multi-indices and
0<p\leq\infty
we define the space
\ell^{p}(\Lambda;\mathcal{Z})
as the set of all
\mathcal{Z}
-valued sequences
\bm{c}=(c_{\bm{\nu}})_{\bm{\nu}\in\Lambda}
for which
{\|\bm{c}\|}_{p;\mathcal{Z}}<\infty
, where

\displaystyle{\|\bm{c}\|}_{p;\mathcal{Z}}=\begin{cases}\left(\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in\Lambda}{\|c_{\bm{\nu}}\|}^{p}_{\mathcal{Z}}\right)^{1/p}&0<p<\infty,\\
\sup_{\bm{\nu}\in\Lambda}{\|c_{\bm{\nu}}\|}_{\mathcal{Z}}&p=\infty.\end{cases}

When
(\mathcal{Z},{\|\cdot\|}_{\mathcal{Z}})=(\mathbb{R},|\cdot|)
, we just write
\ell^{p}(\Lambda)
and
{\|\cdot\|}_{p}
. We also define the following:

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\bm{c}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{p;\mathcal{Z}}=\sup_{z^{*}\in B(\mathcal{Z}^{*})}{\|z^{*}(\bm{c})\|}_{p},\quad\forall\bm{c}\in\ell^{p}(\Lambda;\mathcal{Z}),

where
z^{*}(\bm{c})=(z^{*}(c_{\bm{\nu}}))_{\bm{\nu}\in\Lambda}\in\mathbb{R}^{|\Lambda|}
for
\bm{c}=(c_{\bm{\nu}})_{\bm{\nu}\in\Lambda}
. Notice that
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\bm{c}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{p;\mathcal{Z}}\leq{\|\bm{c}\|}_{p;\mathcal{Z}}
.

Given a sequence
\bm{c}=(c_{\bm{\nu}})_{\bm{\nu}\in\Lambda}
, we write

\mathrm{supp}(\bm{c})=\{\bm{\nu}\in\Lambda:c_{\bm{\nu}}\neq 0\}\subseteq\Lambda.

For
d\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, we write
\bm{e}_{j}
,
j=1,\ldots,d
, for the canonical basis vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We also write
\bm{0}
for the multi-index of zeros and
\bm{1}
for the multi-index of ones.
Finally, for multi-indices
\bm{\nu}=(\nu_{k})^{d}_{k=1}
and
\bm{\mu}=(\mu_{k})^{d}_{k=1}
, we write
\bm{\nu}\geq\bm{\mu}
to mean
\nu_{k}\geq\mu_{k}
,
\forall k
and likewise for
\bm{\nu}>\bm{\mu}
.

C.1.3
Weights and weighted sequence spaces

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
,
\Lambda\subseteq\mathbb{N}^{d}_{0}
and
\bm{w}=(w_{\bm{\nu}})_{\bm{\nu}\in\Lambda}\geq\bm{0}
be a sequence of nonnegative weights. We define the weighted cardinality of a set
S\subseteq\Lambda
as

|S|_{\bm{w}}=\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S}w^{2}_{\bm{\nu}}.

(C.1)

Given a sequence
\bm{c}=(c_{\bm{\nu}})_{\bm{\nu}\in{\Lambda}}
we write

{\|\bm{c}\|}_{0,\bm{w}}=|\mathrm{supp}(\bm{c})|_{\bm{w}}.

Next, for a Banach space
(\mathcal{Z},{\|\cdot\|}_{\mathcal{Z}})
and
0<p\leq 2
, we define the weighted
\ell^{p}_{\bm{w}}({\Lambda};\mathcal{Z})
space as the space of
\mathcal{Z}
-valued sequences
\bm{c}=(c_{\bm{\nu}})_{\bm{\nu}\in{\Lambda}}
for which
{\|\bm{c}\|}_{p,\bm{w};\mathcal{Z}}<\infty
, where

{\|\bm{c}\|}_{p,\bm{u};\mathcal{Z}}=\left(\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in{\Lambda}}w^{2-p}_{\bm{\nu}}{\|c_{\bm{\nu}}\|}^{p}_{\mathcal{Z}}\right)^{1/p}.

Notice that
\|\cdot\|_{2,\bm{u};\mathcal{Z}}
coincides with the unweighted norm
\|\cdot\|_{2;\mathcal{Z}}
.

C.1.4
Legendre polynomials

We write
\{P_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
for the classical Legendre polynomials on
[-1,1]
with normalization
P_{n}(1)=1
. Since
\int^{1}_{-1}|P_{n}(x)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x=(n+1/2)^{-1}
, we define the orthonormal (with respect to the uniform probability measure) Legendre polynomials as

\psi_{n}(x)=\sqrt{2n+1}P_{n}(x),\quad x\in[-1,1],\ n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}.

Let
D=[-1,1]^{\mathbb{N}}
as before,

\mathcal{F}=\left\{\bm{\nu}=(\nu_{i})^{\infty}_{i=1}\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathbb{N}}_{0}:|\mathrm{supp}(\bm{\nu})|<\infty\right\}

be the set of multi-indices with finitely-many nonzero terms and define the multivariate Legendre polynomials as

\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}(\bm{x})=\prod_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\psi_{\nu_{i}}(y_{i})\equiv\prod_{i\in\mathrm{\mathrm{supp}}(\bm{\nu})}\psi_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i}),\quad\forall\bm{x}\in D,\ \bm{\nu}=(\nu_{i})^{\infty}_{i=1}\in\mathcal{F}.

Here, the second equality follows from the fact that
\psi_{0}=1
. Then it is known that the set

\{\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}\}_{\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{F}}\subset L^{2}_{\varrho}(D)

(C.2)

constitutes an orthonormal basis for
L^{2}_{\varrho}(D)

[
19
, §3]
. For later use, we also define the sequences

\bm{u}=(u_{\bm{\nu}})_{\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{F}},\quad\text{where }u_{\bm{\nu}}={\|\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D)}=\prod_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\sqrt{2\nu_{k}+1},\ \forall\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{F},

(C.3)

and

\bm{v}=(v_{\bm{\nu}})_{\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{F}},\quad\text{where }v_{\bm{\nu}}=u^{5+\xi}_{\bm{\nu}},\ \forall\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{F}.

(C.4)

Here
\xi\geq 0
will be chosen later in the proof.

C.1.5
Miscellaneous

Given an optimization problem
\min_{t}f(t)
, we say that
\hat{t}
is a
(\sigma,\tau)
-approximate minimizer
for some
\sigma\geq 1
and
\tau\geq 0
if
f(\hat{t})\leq\sigma^{2}\min_{t}f(t)+\tau^{2}
. (In the main paper, we consider only
\sigma=1
, but for the proofs it is useful to allow
\sigma>1
).

Finally, for convenience, given
X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}\in\mathcal{X}
, we define the semi-norm

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|G|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}=\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|G(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}

of an operator
G:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
and

{\|g\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}={|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|g\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}=\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|g\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}

of a function
g:\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
. Here, as in (A.III),
\tilde{\varsigma}
denotes the pushforward measure
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\#\mu
.

C.2
Overview of the proofs

C.2.1
Theorem
3.1

Theorems
3.1
-
3.2
are based on polynomials, and specifically, procedures for learning efficient Legendre polynomial approximations to holomorphic operators. As observed, these results are based on recent work on learning holomorphic, Banach-valued functions
[
2
,
5
,
6
]
. See also Remark
C.2.1
below.

The proof of Theorem
3.1
involves three mains steps.

(a)

Formulation (§
D.1
) and analysis (§
D.2
-
D.4
) of a suitable polynomial learning procedure.

(b)

Construction of a family of DNNs that approximately emulates the Legendre polynomials (§
D.5
).

(c)

Analysis of the corresponding training problem (
2.5
) (§
D.6
–
D.8
).

Since our goal in this work is ‘agnostic’ DNN architectures (i.e., independent of the smoothness of the underlying operator), in step (a) we first define a nonlinear set (
D.2
) spanned by Legendre polynomials with nonzero indices in certain sets of bounded weighted cardinality. This is effectively a form of
sparse polynomial approximation
, and the analysis of the resulting learning procedure (
D.3
) relies heavily on techniques from compressed sensing. In order to bound the encoding-decoding error, we also require several results on Lipschitz continuity (Lemma
D.2
) and norm equivalences (Lemma
D.4
) for multivariate polynomials.

Step (b) relies on what have now become fairly standard results in DNN approximation theory: namely, the approximate emulation of orthogonal polynomials via DNNs of given width and depth. We present such a result in Lemma
D.9
, then use this to define the DNN family
\mathcal{N}
in (
D.19
).

We then analyze the DNN training problem (
2.5
) in step (c). Using the emulation result of step (b), we first show that any approximate minimizer
\widehat{N}
of (
2.5
) yields a polynomial that is also an approximate minimizer of the polynomial training problem (
D.3
) (Lemma
D.12
). We may then apply the results shown in Step (a) to prove a generalization bound (Theorem
D.13
). Up to this point, we have not used the holomorphy assumption. We now use this assumption to bound the various best polynomial approximation errors that arise in the previously-derived generalization bound (§
D.7
). Finally, in §
D.8
we put all these estimates together to complete the proof.

Theorem
3.1
is a generalization and improvement of
[
5
, Thms. 4.1 & 4.2]
, which deals with the case of learning Banach-valued functions rather than operators. Specifically, the setting of
[
5
]
can be considered a special case of this paper where
\mathcal{X}=\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{N})
and the encoding error
E_{\mathcal{X},q}=0
. Moreover, Theorem
3.1
improves the main results of
[
5
]
in three key ways. First, the the DNN architectures bounds are much narrower:
\mathrm{width}(\mathcal{N})\lesssim(m/L)^{1+\delta}
versus
\mathrm{width}(\mathcal{N})\lesssim m^{3+\log_{2}(m)}
in the latter. Second, Theorem
3.1
considers standard training, i.e.,
\ell^{2}
-loss minimization, whereas
[
5
, Thms. 4.1 & 4.2]
requires regularization. Third, for Banach spaces, the error decay rate with respect to
m
is roughly doubled:
E_{\mathsf{app},2}=\mathcal{O}(m^{1-1/p})
in Theorem
3.1
versus
\mathcal{O}(m^{1/2(1-1/p)})
in
[
5
, Thm. 4.1]
. Finally, Theorem
3.1
also provides bounds in the
L^{\infty}_{\mu}
-norm, whereas the results in
[
5
]
only consider the
L^{2}_{\mu}
-norm.

C.2.2
Theorem
3.2

The proof of Theorem
3.2
relies of three key steps.

(a)

Using a minimizer of the polynomial training problem (
D.3
) to construct a family of minimizers of the DNN training problem (
2.5
).

(b)

Analysis of the corresponding minimizers using the previously-derived bound for polynomial minimizers.

(c)

Using the Lipschitz continuity of DNNs to show stability of the DNN minimizer and a permutation argument to show the existence of many parameters that lead to equally ‘good’ minimizers.

Given any minimizer of the polynomial training problem (
D.3
), it is straightforward to define a DNN with the desired generalization bound. Unfortunately, this will generally not be a minimizer of (
2.5
). To achieve the aims of step (a) we proceed as follows. First, we note that a DNN will be a minimizer if the corresponding approximation satisfies
\widehat{F}(X_{i})=\widetilde{Y}_{i}
, where
\widetilde{Y}_{i}
are the closest points to the
Y_{i}
from
\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}=\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}})
. To achieve this, we take the existing DNN then add on a suitable number of additional terms corresponding to the first
r>m
order-one Legendre polynomials (§
E.1
). We show that by doing this, we can construct a DNN for which
\widehat{F}(X_{i})=\widetilde{Y}_{i}
(Lemma
E.1
).

In Step (b), we first bound this DNN minimizer in terms of the polynomial minimizer plus the contributions of these additional terms (Lemma
E.2
). The latter involves the minimal singular value of a certain
m\times r
matrix
\bm{B}
, which is the matrix of the linear system that enforces the condition
\widehat{F}(X_{i})=\widetilde{Y}_{i}
. We bound this minimal singular value in §
E.3
.

We then use this to complete the proof of part (A) of Theorem
3.2
in §
E.4
. In this section, we also complete step (c) to establish parts (B) and (C).

Like Theorem
3.1
, Theorem
3.2
also relies on ideas from
[
5
]
. However,
[
5
]
does not address fully-connected DNN architectures. To address this challenge, the proof of Theorem
3.2
involves the technical construction described above.

C.2.3
Theorem
4.1

Theorems
4.1
is based on
[
7
, Thm. 4.4]
. The basic idea is to consider a family of affine, holomorphic operators (
F.4
). This allows us to lower bound the quantity
\theta_{m}(\bm{b})
by the so-called
Gelfand width
(
F.1
) of a certain weighted unit ball in a finite-dimensional space. Bounds for such Gelfand widths are known, and this allows us to derive the corresponding result.
The main difference between this and
[
7
, Thm. 4.4]
is the setup leading to the construction in (
F.4
).

C.2.4
Theorem
4.2

Theorems
4.2
employs similar ideas, but in a more technical manner. We consider a family of linear, holomorphic operators (
G.2
), which involves a sum over
r
groups of
m+1
coefficients. We restrict to coefficients that lie in the null space of the corresponding sampling operator. Then, through a series of inequalities, we can lower bound
\tilde{\theta}_{m}(\bm{b})
by a sum over
r
terms, each involving the
\ell^{1}
-norms of certain vectors in the null space of the matrix of the corresponding sampling operator (
G.3
). This matrix is a subgaussian random matrix. We now use a technical estimate from
[
75
]
for vectors in the null space of subgaussian random matrices, which shows that they cannot be ‘spiky’. Applying this and a series of further inequalities yields the result.

Appendix D
Proof of Theorem
3.1

D.1
Formulation of an approximate polynomial training problem

Let
\Lambda\subset\mathcal{F}
with
\mathrm{supp}(\bm{\nu})\subseteq\{1,\ldots,d_{\mathcal{X}}\}
,
\forall\bm{\nu}\in\Lambda
, and write
N=|\Lambda|
. Let
k>0
and define the set

\mathcal{S}=\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda,k}=\{S\subseteq\Lambda:|S|_{\bm{v}}\leq k\}.

(D.1)

Both
\Lambda
and
k
will be chosen later in the proof. For any Banach space
(\mathcal{Z},{\|\cdot\|}_{\mathcal{Z}})
define the space

\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S};\mathcal{Z}}=\left\{\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S}c_{\bm{\nu}}\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}:c_{\bm{\nu}}\in\mathcal{Z},S\in\mathcal{S}\right\}.

(D.2)

Then, given the training data (
2.4
), consider the problem

\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S};\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|Y_{i}-p\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}},

(D.3)

where
\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}=\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}})
. Here and throughout the proofs, we slightly abuse notation: the polynomial
p:\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, whereas
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}
has codomain
\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}
. However, by construction,
p
is independent of all but the first
d_{\mathcal{X}}
variables. Hence we may consider
p
as a function
\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
.
This aside, if
\hat{p}
is an approximate minimizer of (
D.3
), then we define the approximation to
F
as

F\approx\widehat{F}=\hat{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}.

(D.4)

Notice that
p\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}=\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ P\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}
, where
P:\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}
is a vector-valued polynomial, since, by (A.IV), the map
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}
is linear. The idea exploited in this proof is to construct a class of DNNs
\mathcal{N}
such that (i) all such polynomials
P
are approximated by members of
\mathcal{N}
and (ii) the polynomial training problem (
D.3
) is approximated by the DNN training problem (
2.5
).
The first step in this analysis is therefore to analyze the polynomial training problem (
D.3
).

D.2
Supporting lemmas

We require several lemmas. The first relates the
L^{\infty}_{\varrho}
-norm of a polynomial to its Pettis
L^{2}_{\varrho}
-norm.

Lemma D.1

(Nikolskii inequality for polynomials)
.

Let
(\mathcal{Z},{\|\cdot\|}_{\mathcal{Z}})
be any Banach space and
p=\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S}c_{\bm{\nu}}\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}
for some finite set
S\subset\mathcal{F}
, where
c_{\bm{\nu}}\in\mathcal{Z}
. Then

{\|p\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Z})}\leq\sqrt{|S|_{\bm{u}}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Z})}.

Proof.

By definition

{\|p\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Z})}={|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Z})}=\sup_{z^{*}\in B(\mathcal{Z}^{*})}{\|z^{*}(p)\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D)}.

Fix
z^{*}\in B(\mathcal{Z}^{*})
and write
z^{*}(p)=\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S}z^{*}(c_{\bm{\nu}})\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}
. By the triangle inequality and the definition of the weights (
C.3
), we have

{\|z^{*}(p)\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D)}\leq\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S}|z^{*}(c_{\bm{\nu}})|{\|\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D)}=\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S}u_{\bm{\nu}}|z^{*}(c_{\bm{\nu}})|.

We now apply the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, (
C.1
) and Parseval’s identity to get

{\|z^{*}(p)\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D)}\leq\sqrt{|S|_{\bm{u}}}\sqrt{\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S}|z^{*}(c_{\bm{\nu}})|^{2}}=\sqrt{|S|_{\bm{u}}}{\|z^{*}(p)\|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D)}.

Since
z^{*}
was arbitrary, we deduce that

{\|p\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Z})}\leq\sqrt{|S|_{\bm{u}}}\sup_{z^{*}\in B(\mathcal{Z}^{*})}{\|z^{*}(p)\|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D)}=\sqrt{|S|_{\bm{u}}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Z})},

as required.
∎

Next, we require the following bound on the Lipschitz constant of a multivariate polynomial.

Lemma D.2

(Lipschitz continuity for polynomials)
.

Let
(\mathcal{Z},{\|\cdot\|}_{\mathcal{Z}})
be any Banach space and suppose that
p=\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S}c_{\bm{\nu}}\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}
for some finite
S\subset\mathcal{F}
, where
c_{\bm{\nu}}\in\mathcal{Z}
. Then satisfies

\mathrm{Lip}(p;B^{\infty}(\mathbb{N}),\mathcal{Z})\leq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{|S|_{\bm{v}}}\cdot{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Z})},

where
B^{\infty}(\mathbb{N})=\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}}:{\|\bm{x}\|}_{\infty}\leq 1\}
is the unit ball of
\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{N})
.

Proof.

Fix
z^{*}\in B(\mathcal{Z}^{*})
and let
\tilde{p}=z^{*}(p)=\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S}z^{*}(c_{\bm{\nu}})\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}=:\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S}\tilde{c}_{\bm{\nu}}\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}
be the corresponding scalar-valued polynomial. Let
\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime}\in D
. Then the mean value theorem gives that

\tilde{p}(\bm{x}^{\prime})-\tilde{p}(\bm{x})=\sum^{\infty}_{i=1}(x^{\prime}_{i}-x_{i})\frac{\partial\tilde{p}}{\partial x_{i}}(t\bm{x}+(1-t)\bm{x}^{\prime})

for some
0\leq t\leq 1
. Since
B^{\infty}(\mathbb{N})\equiv D
, this and the fact that
D
is convex give that

|\tilde{p}(\bm{x}^{\prime})-\tilde{p}(\bm{x})|\leq{\|\bm{x}^{\prime}-\bm{x}\|}_{\infty}\sum^{\infty}_{i=1}{\left\|\frac{\partial\tilde{p}}{\partial x_{i}}\right\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D)}.

(D.5)

We now consider the terms in the sum separately. Using the definition of the Legendre polynomials (see §
C.1.4
), we have

\frac{\partial\tilde{p}}{\partial x_{i}}=\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S}\tilde{c}_{\bm{\nu}}u_{\bm{\nu}}P^{\prime}_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i})\prod_{j\neq i}P_{\nu_{j}}(x_{j}).

The unnormalized Legendre polynomials satisy
1=P_{n}(1)={\|P_{n}\|}_{L^{\infty}([-1,1])}
and
n(n+1)/2=P^{\prime}_{n}(1)={\|P^{\prime}_{n}\|}_{L^{\infty}([-1,1])}
. Hence

{\left\|\frac{\partial\tilde{p}}{\partial x_{i}}\right\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D)}\leq\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S}|\tilde{c}_{\bm{\nu}}|u_{\bm{\nu}}\nu_{i}(\nu_{i}+1)/2.

We deduce that

\displaystyle\sum^{\infty}_{i=1}{\left\|\frac{\partial\tilde{p}}{\partial x_{i}}\right\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D)}\leq\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S}|\tilde{c}_{\bm{\nu}}|u_{\bm{\nu}}\sum^{\infty}_{i=1}\nu_{i}(\nu_{i}+1)/2.

Now, by definition of the weights
u_{\bm{\nu}}
and
v_{\bm{\nu}}
(see (
C.3
) and (
C.4
)), we have

(\nu_{i}+1)\leq\prod_{j\in\mathbb{N}}(2\nu_{j}+1)=u^{2}_{\bm{\nu}}

and

\sum^{\infty}_{i=1}\nu_{i}\leq\prod_{j\in\mathbb{N}}(2\nu_{j}+1)=u^{2}_{\bm{\nu}}.

Hence

\sum^{\infty}_{i=1}u_{\bm{\nu}}\nu_{i}(\nu_{i}+1)\leq u^{5}_{\bm{\nu}}\leq v_{\bm{\nu}}.

Here we also used the fact that
\bm{u}\geq\bm{1}
.
We now apply this, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Parseval’s identity to get

\sum^{\infty}_{i=1}{\left\|\frac{\partial\tilde{p}}{\partial x_{i}}\right\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D)}\leq\frac{1}{2}{\|\tilde{p}\|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D)}\sqrt{|S|_{\bm{v}}}.

Substituting this into (
D.5
) now gives

|\tilde{p}(\bm{x}^{\prime})-\tilde{p}(\bm{x})|\leq\frac{1}{2}{\|\tilde{p}\|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D)}\sqrt{|S|_{\bm{v}}}{\|\bm{x}^{\prime}-\bm{x}\|}_{\infty}.

We now recall that
\tilde{p}=z^{*}(p)
and
z^{*}\in B(\mathcal{Z}^{*})
was arbitrary to get

\displaystyle{\|p(\bm{x}^{\prime})-p(\bm{x})\|}_{\mathcal{Z}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{z^{*}\in B(\mathcal{Z}^{*})}|z^{*}(p(\bm{x}^{\prime})-p(\bm{x}))|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\sup_{z^{*}\in B(\mathcal{Z}^{*})}{\|z^{*}(p)\|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D)}\sqrt{|S|_{\bm{v}}}{\|\bm{x}^{\prime}-\bm{x}\|}_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Z})}\sqrt{|S|_{\bm{v}}}{\|\bm{x}^{\prime}-\bm{x}\|}_{\infty},

as required.
∎

We now show that this result can be used to imply a norm equivalence for polynomials. For this we first require the following lemma. Note that in this and subsequent results, we abuse notation and write
\tilde{\varsigma}
for both the measure on
[-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}
defined in (A.III) and the measure on
D=[-1,1]^{\mathbb{N}}
defined by tensoring this measure (corresponding to the first
d_{\mathcal{X}}
variables
x_{1},\ldots,x_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}
) with the uniform measure on
D\backslash[-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}
(corresponding to the remaining variables
x_{d_{\mathcal{X}}+1},x_{d_{\mathcal{X}}+2},\ldots
).

Lemma D.3

(Closeness of
L^{2}
norms)
.

Let
(\mathcal{Z},{\|\cdot\|}_{\mathcal{Z}})
be any Banach space,
\varsigma
be the measure defined in (A.I) and
\tilde{\varsigma}
be the measure defined in (A.III). Suppose that
f\in L^{2}_{\varsigma}(D;\mathcal{Z})
is Lipschitz continuous with constant
L=\mathrm{Lip}(f;B^{\infty}(\mathbb{N}),\mathcal{Z})<\infty
and that
f
depends only on its first
d_{\mathcal{X}}
variables.
Then
f\in L^{2}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Z})
and

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varsigma}(D;\mathcal{Z})}-\delta\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Z})}\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varsigma}(D;\mathcal{Z})}+\delta,

where

\delta=L\cdot{\|\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}-\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{X}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{N}))}.

Proof.

Fix
z^{*}\in B(\mathcal{Z}^{*})
, let
g=z^{*}(f)\in L^{2}_{\varsigma}(D)
and set

a={\|g\|}_{L^{2}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D)},\qquad b={\|g\|}_{L^{2}_{\varsigma}(D)}.

Notice that

|g(\bm{x})-g(\bm{x}^{\prime})|\leq{\|z^{*}\|}_{\mathcal{Z}^{*}}{\|f(\bm{x})-f(\bm{x}^{\prime})\|}_{\mathcal{Z}}\leq L{\|\bm{x}-\bm{x}^{\prime}\|}_{\infty},\quad\forall\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime}\in B^{\infty}(\mathbb{N}).

Now, with slight abuse of notation,
g(\iota(X))=g(\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}(X))
due to the assumption on
f
. Therefore, using this and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle a^{2}-b^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{D}(g(\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{X}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}}(X)))^{2}-(g(\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}(X)))^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mu(X)

\displaystyle\leq L\int_{D}{\|\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}(X)-\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{X}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}}(X)\|}_{\infty}\left(|g(\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}(X))|+|g(\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{X}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}})|\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(X)

\displaystyle\leq L{\|\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}-\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{X}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{N}))}\left(a+b\right).

We deduce that
a-b\leq\delta
. Since
z^{*}
was arbitrary, we get

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Z})}=\sup_{z^{*}\in B(\mathcal{Z}^{*})}{\|z^{*}(f)\|}_{L^{2}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D)}\leq\sup_{z^{*}\in B(\mathcal{Z}^{*})}{\|z^{*}(f)\|}_{L^{2}_{\varsigma}(D)}+\delta={|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varsigma}(D;\mathcal{Z})}+\delta,

which gives the upper bound.
The same argument applied to
b^{2}-a^{2}
also gives the lower bound.
∎

Lemma D.4

(Norm equivalences for polynomials)
.

Let
(\mathcal{Z},{\|\cdot\|}_{\mathcal{Z}})
be any Banach space,
\varsigma
be the measure defined in (A.I),
\tilde{\varsigma}
be the measure defined in (A.III) and
S\subset\mathcal{F}
with
\mathrm{supp}(\bm{\nu})\in\{1,\ldots,d_{\mathcal{X}}\}
,
\forall\bm{\nu}\in S
. Suppose that

\sqrt{|S|_{\bm{v}}}\cdot{\|\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}-\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{X}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{N}))}\leq c,

(D.6)

for some sufficiently small universal constant
c>0
. Then the norm equivalence

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Z})}\lesssim{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Z})}\lesssim{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Z})}

holds for all
p=\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S}c_{\bm{\nu}}\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}
, where
c_{\bm{\nu}}\in\mathcal{Z}
.

Proof.

Combining Lemmas
D.2
and
D.3
, we see that

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varsigma}(D;\mathcal{Z})}-\delta\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Z})}\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varsigma}(D;\mathcal{Z})}+\delta,

where
\delta=\frac{1}{2}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Z})}\sqrt{|S|_{\bm{v}}}{\|\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}-\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{X}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{N}))}\leq c{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Z})}/2
. By (A.I), there are constants
c_{1}\geq c_{2}>0
such that

c_{1}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Z})}\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varsigma}(D;\mathcal{Z})}\leq c_{2}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Z})}.

We now take
c=c_{1}/2
.
∎

D.3
Analysis of (
D.3
)

We now analyze (
D.3
). Our analysis relies on the following result, which shows an error bound subject to a certain discrete metric inequality (
D.7
).

Lemma D.5

(Discrete metric inequality implies error bounds)
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}:\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}=\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}})
be a bounded linear operator and
\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}={\|\mathcal{Q}\|}_{\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}}
. Suppose that

{\|p-q\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}\geq\alpha\max\{{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p-q|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p-q|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\},\quad\forall p,q\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S};\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}}

(D.7)

for some
\alpha>0
.
Then, for any
F\in L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})
,
p\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S};\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}}
and
q\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S};\mathcal{Y}}
, we have

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-p\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\leq

\displaystyle~{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\alpha^{-1}{\|p-\mathcal{Q}\circ q\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}

\displaystyle+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}

and

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-p\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\leq

\displaystyle~{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{2k}/\alpha{\|p-\mathcal{Q}\circ q\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}

\displaystyle+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}{\|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}.

Proof.

By the triangle inequality and properties of
\mathcal{Q}
, we have

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-p\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}

\displaystyle\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}-\mathcal{Q}\circ q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{Q}\circ F-\mathcal{Q}\circ q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}

\displaystyle\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}-\mathcal{Q}\circ q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}.

Now, since
p,Q\circ q\in\mathcal{P}_{S;\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}}
, the second term can be bounded by

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}-\mathcal{Q}\circ q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}={|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p-\mathcal{Q}\circ q|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\leq\alpha^{-1}{\|p-\mathcal{Q}\circ q\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}.

(D.8)

This yields the first result.

For the second result, we once more write

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-p\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\leq

\displaystyle~{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}-\mathcal{Q}\circ q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}

\displaystyle+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}.

For the second term, we use (A.III) to write

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}-\mathcal{Q}\circ q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}={|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p-\mathcal{Q}\circ q|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{\infty}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\leq{\|p-\mathcal{Q}\circ q\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}.

Notice that
p-\mathcal{Q}\circ q
is a polynomial supported in a set
S
with
|S|_{\bm{u}}\leq 2k
.
We now apply Lemma
D.1
and (
D.7
) to obtain

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}-\mathcal{Q}\circ q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\leq\sqrt{2k}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p-\mathcal{Q}\circ q|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\leq\sqrt{2k}/\alpha{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p-\mathcal{Q}\circ q|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}.

(D.9)

This gives the result.
∎

Theorem D.6

(Error bound for polynomial minimizers)
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}:\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}=\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}})
be a bounded linear operator,
\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}={\|\mathcal{Q}\|}_{\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}}
and suppose that (
D.7
) holds. Let
\widehat{F}
be as in (
D.4
) for some
(\sigma,\tau)
-approximate minimizer
\hat{p}
of (
D.3
). Then

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\widehat{F}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\leq

\displaystyle~{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\frac{\sigma+1}{\alpha}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}

\displaystyle+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}\left({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\frac{\sigma+1}{\alpha}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\tau}{\alpha}+\frac{\sigma+1}{\alpha\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}

and

\displaystyle{\|F-\widehat{F}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\leq

\displaystyle~{\|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\frac{\sqrt{2k}(\sigma+1)}{\alpha}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}

\displaystyle+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}\left({\|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\frac{\sqrt{2k}(\sigma+1)}{\alpha}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\sqrt{2k}\tau}{\alpha}+\frac{\sqrt{2k}(\sigma+1)}{\alpha\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}

for all
q\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S};\mathcal{Y}}
, where
\bm{E}=(E_{i})^{m}_{i=1}\in\mathcal{Y}^{m}
is the (Banach-valued) vector of noise terms.

Proof.

We apply the previous lemma with
p=\hat{p}
. This gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\widehat{F}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\leq&~{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\alpha^{-1}{\|\hat{p}-\mathcal{Q}\circ q\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}\\
&+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\\
{\|F-\widehat{F}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\leq&~{\|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{2k}/\alpha{\|\hat{p}-\mathcal{Q}\circ q\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}\\
&+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}{\|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}.\end{split}

(D.10)

Consider the second term. We have

\displaystyle{\|\hat{p}-\mathcal{Q}\circ q\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}

\displaystyle={|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}-\mathcal{Q}\circ q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\hat{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc,\mu}}.

Consider the second term of this expression. By the triangle inequality and the facts that
\hat{p}
is a
(\sigma,\tau)
-minimizer and
\mathcal{Q}\circ q
is feasible, we obtain

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\hat{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc,\mu}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|Y_{i}-\hat{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}

\displaystyle\leq\sigma\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|Y_{i}-\mathcal{Q}\circ q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}+\tau+\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}

\displaystyle\leq\sigma{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}+\tau+\frac{\sigma+1}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}

Therefore, we get

\displaystyle{\|\hat{p}-\mathcal{Q}\circ q\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}\leq(\sigma+1){|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}+\tau+\frac{\sigma+1}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}.

We now estimate the first term in this expression as follows:

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}

\displaystyle\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{Q}\circ F-\mathcal{Q}\circ q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}

\displaystyle\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}.

Therefore, we conclude that

{\|\hat{p}-\mathcal{Q}\circ q\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}\leq(\sigma+1){|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}+(\sigma+1)\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}+\tau+\frac{\sigma+1}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}.

Combining this with (
D.10
) now gives the result.
∎

D.4
Ensuring (
D.7
) holds with high probability

For the proof of the next lemma and subsequent steps of the proof, we let
\Lambda=\{\bm{\nu}_{1},\ldots,\bm{\nu}_{N}\}
and define the matrix

\bm{A}=\left(\frac{\Psi_{\bm{\nu}_{j}}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i}))}{\sqrt{m}}\right)^{m,N}_{i,j=1}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times N}.

Lemma D.7
.

Let
0<\epsilon<1
,
0<\delta<1
,
k>0
and suppose that

m\geq c_{0}\cdot\delta^{-2}\cdot k\cdot(\log(\mathrm{e}N)\cdot\log^{2}(k/\delta)+\log(2/\epsilon))

(D.11)

for some universal constant
c_{0}>0
. Let
T=\{\bm{c}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}:{\|\bm{c}\|}_{2}=1,\ {\|\bm{c}\|}_{0,\bm{v}}\leq k\}
and

\theta_{+}=\sup\left\{\mathbb{E}{\|\bm{A}\bm{c}\|}^{2}_{2}:\bm{c}\in T\right\},\quad\theta_{-}=\inf\left\{\mathbb{E}{\|\bm{A}\bm{c}\|}^{2}_{2}:\bm{c}\in T\right\}.

Then

{\|\bm{A}\bm{c}\|}^{2}_{2}\geq\left(\theta_{-}-(1+\theta_{+})c_{1}\delta\right){\|\bm{c}\|}^{2}_{2},\quad\forall\bm{c}\in\mathbb{R}^{N},\ {\|\bm{c}\|}_{0,\bm{v}}\leq k.

with probability at least
1-\epsilon
, where
c_{1}>0
is a universal constant.

Proof.

The proof is based on
[
12
, Thm. 2.13]
. Since
{\|\bm{c}\|}_{1,\bm{v}}\leq\sqrt{{\|\bm{c}\|}_{0,\bm{v}}}{\|\bm{c}\|}_{2}\leq\sqrt{k}
, we see that

T\subseteq\left\{\bm{c}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}:{\|\bm{c}\|}_{1,\bm{v}}\leq\sqrt{k}\right\}.

Define the random vector
\bm{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
by
\bm{X}=(\Psi_{\bm{\nu}_{i}}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X)))^{N}_{i=1}
for
X\sim\mu
. Observe that

|\langle\bm{X},\bm{e}_{j}\rangle|=|\Psi_{\bm{\nu}_{i}}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X))|\leq u_{\bm{\nu}_{i}}\leq v_{\bm{\nu}_{i}}

almost surely,
by (A.III) and the definition of the weights. Therefore, by
[
12
, Thm. 2.13]
, if

m\geq c_{0}\cdot\delta^{-2}\cdot k\cdot\log(\mathrm{e}N)\cdot\log^{2}(k/(c_{1}\delta)),

(D.12)

it holds that

\sup_{\bm{c}\in T}\left|\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}|\langle\bm{c},\bm{X}_{i}\rangle|^{2}-\mathbb{E}|\langle\bm{c},\bm{X}\rangle|^{2}\right|\leq c_{1}\delta\left(1+\sup_{\bm{c}\in T}\mathbb{E}|\langle\bm{c},\bm{X}\rangle|^{2}\right)

with probability at least
1-2\exp(-c_{2}\delta^{2}m/k)
. Here
c_{0},c_{1},c_{2}>0
are universal constants. Now observe that

\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}|\langle\bm{c},\bm{X}_{i}\rangle|^{2}={\|\bm{A}\bm{c}\|}^{2}_{2},\qquad\mathbb{E}|\langle\bm{c},\bm{X}\rangle|^{2}=\mathbb{E}({\|\bm{A}\bm{c}\|}^{2}_{2}).

Therefore, we have shown that

{\|\bm{A}\bm{c}\|}^{2}_{2}\geq\theta_{-}-(1+\theta_{+})c_{1}\delta,\quad\forall\bm{c}\in T,

with probability at least
1-2\exp(-c_{2}\delta^{2}m/k)
, provided
m
satisfies (
D.12
). To conclude the result, we observe that (
D.11
) implies (
D.12
), up to a possible change in the universal constant
c_{0}
. Moreover, it also implies that

2\exp(-c_{2}\delta^{2}m/k)\leq\epsilon.

Hence we obtain the result.
∎

Lemma D.8
.

There exist universal constants
c_{0},c_{1},c_{2}>0
such that the following holds.
Suppose that

m\geq c_{0}\cdot k\cdot(\log(\mathrm{e}N)\cdot\log^{2}(k)+\log(2/\epsilon))

(D.13)

and

\sqrt{k}\cdot{\|\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}-\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{X}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{N}))}\leq c_{1}.

(D.14)

Then (
D.7
) holds with probability at least
1-\epsilon
and constant
\alpha\geq c_{2}
.

Proof.

We shall apply the previous lemma with
k
replaced by
2k
. First, we estimate
\theta_{+}
and
\theta_{-}
. Let
\bm{c}\in T
, with
T
as defined therein with
2k
in place of
k
. Write
p=\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in\Lambda}c_{\bm{\nu}}\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}
for the corresponding (scalar-valued) polynomial. Then

{\|\bm{A}\bm{c}\|}^{2}_{2}=\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}|p\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})|^{2},

and therefore

\mathbb{E}{\|\bm{A}\bm{c}\|}^{2}_{2}={\|p\|}^{2}_{L^{2}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D)}.

Since
{\|\bm{c}\|}_{0,\bm{v}}\leq 2k
, (
D.14
) implies (
D.6
). We now apply Lemma
D.4
and the fact that
{\|p\|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D)}={\|\bm{c}\|}_{2}=1
to get

c_{3}\leq\mathbb{E}{\|\bm{A}\bm{c}\|}^{2}_{2}\leq c_{4}

for universal constants
c_{4}\geq c_{3}>0
.
Since
\bm{c}\in T
was arbitrary to deduce that

c_{3}\leq\theta_{-}\leq\theta_{+}\leq c_{4}.

(D.15)

We now show that (
D.7
) holds with the desired probability. Let
p,q\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S};\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}}
be arbitrary. Then their difference
h=p-q
can be expressed as

h=\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in\Lambda}d_{\bm{\nu}}\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}

(D.16)

where
\bm{d}=(d_{\bm{\nu}})_{\bm{\nu}\in\Lambda}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}^{N}
satisfies

{\|\bm{d}\|}_{0,\bm{u}}\leq{\|\bm{d}\|}_{0,\bm{v}}\leq 2k.

Now, this, (
D.14
) and Lemma
D.4
imply that

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\leq c_{4}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}.

Therefore, in order to prove (
D.7
), it suffices to show that
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim{\|h\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}
.

Observe that

{\|h\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}={\|\bm{A}\bm{d}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}\geq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\bm{A}\bm{d}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}=\sup_{y^{*}\in B(\mathcal{Y}^{*})}{\|y^{*}(\bm{A}\bm{d})\|}_{2},

where we recall that for a vector
\bm{z}=(z_{i})^{N}_{i=1}\in\mathcal{Y}^{N}
, we write
y^{*}(\bm{z})=(y^{*}(z_{i}))^{N}_{i=1}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
. By linearity, we have
y^{*}(\bm{A}\bm{d})=\bm{A}y^{*}(\bm{d})
. Therefore, by Lemma
D.7
,

\displaystyle{\|h\|}^{2}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}

\displaystyle={\|\bm{A}\bm{d}\|}^{2}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}

\displaystyle\geq\sup_{y^{*}\in B(\mathcal{Y}^{*})}{\|\bm{A}y^{*}(\bm{d})\|}^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sup_{y^{*}\in B(\mathcal{Y}^{*})}\left(\theta_{-}-(1+\theta_{+})c_{5}\delta\right){\|y^{*}(\bm{d})\|}^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\left(c_{3}-(1+c_{4})c_{5}\delta\right)\sup_{y^{*}\in B(\mathcal{Y}^{*})}{\|y^{*}(h)\|}^{2}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D)}

\displaystyle=\left(c_{3}-(1+c_{4})c_{5}\delta\right){|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}^{2}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}.

for some universal constant
c_{5}>0
, provided
m
satisfies (
D.11
). We now set
\delta=c_{3}/(2(1+c_{4})c_{5})
to get

{\|h\|}^{2}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}\geq c_{3}/2{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}^{2}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})},

provided
m
satisfies (
D.11
) with this value of
\delta
.
However, this is implied by the condition (
D.13
). We deduce the result.
∎

D.5
Construction of the DNN family
\mathcal{N}

Recall that
\Lambda\subset\mathcal{F}
,
|\Lambda|=N
is an arbitrary set and
\mathcal{S}
is defined by (
D.1
). In this and what follows, we slightly abuse notation and consider a DNN
N:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
as a function
\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
which depends on only the first
d
variables.

Lemma D.9

(Approximating Legendre polynomials with tanh DNNs)
.

Let
\Gamma\subset\Lambda
with
\mathrm{supp}(\bm{\nu})\subseteq\{1,\ldots,d\}
,
\forall\bm{\nu}\in\Gamma
, and
m(\Gamma)=\max_{\bm{\nu}\in\Gamma}{\|\bm{\nu}\|}_{1}<\infty
. There exists a fully-connected family
\mathcal{N}_{o}
of tanh DNNs
N:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with

\mathrm{width}(\mathcal{N}_{o})\lesssim m(\Gamma),\quad\mathrm{depth}(\mathcal{N}_{o})\lesssim\log(m(\Gamma)),

such that, for any
0<\delta<1
and
\bm{\nu}\in\Gamma
, there is a DNN
N_{\bm{\nu}}\in\mathcal{N}_{o}
with

{\|N_{\bm{\nu}}-\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D)}\leq\delta.

(D.17)

Moreover, the zero network
0:\bm{x}\mapsto 0
also belongs to
\mathcal{N}_{o}
(trivially, since
\tanh(0)=0
).

Proof.

We follow the argument of
[
5
, Thm. 7.4]
, which is based on
[
79
, Prop. 2.6]
. Let
\bm{\nu}\in\Gamma
. Then via the fundamental theorem of algebra we can write
\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}(\bm{x})
as a product of
{\|\bm{\nu}\|}_{1}
numbers as

\displaystyle\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}(\bm{x})=\prod_{i\in\mathrm{supp}(\bm{\nu})}\prod^{\nu_{i}}_{j=1}d_{i}(x_{i}-r_{ij}).

Here
\{r_{ij}\}^{\nu_{i}}_{j=1}
are the roots of the univariate Legendre polynomial
P_{\nu_{i}}
. We append
m(\Gamma)-{\|\bm{\nu}\|}_{1}
ones and write
\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}(\bm{x})
as a product of exactly
m(\Gamma)
numbers. Now define the affine map

\mathcal{A}_{\bm{\nu}}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{m(\Gamma)},

(D.18)

so that
\mathcal{A}_{\bm{\nu}}(\bm{x})
is the vector consisting of the values
d_{i}(x_{i}-r_{ij})
for
j=1,\ldots,\nu_{i}
and
i\in\mathrm{supp}(\bm{\nu})
and
1
otherwise. To complete the proof, we need to construct a tanh DNN mapping
\mathbb{R}^{m(\Gamma)}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
that approximately multiplies these numbers. To do this, we argue as in the proof of
[
5
, Thm. 7.4]
to see that there is a tanh DNN
N_{\bm{\nu}}
with

\mathrm{width}(N_{\bm{\nu}})\lesssim m(\Gamma),\quad\mathrm{depth}(N_{\bm{\nu}})\lesssim\log(m(\Gamma))

that satisfies the desired bound (
D.17
). Since these width and depth bounds are independent of
\bm{\nu}
, we deduce the result.
∎

Fix
\delta>0
, let
\Gamma=\cup_{S\in\mathcal{S}}S
,
d=d_{\mathcal{X}}
and consider the corresponding family
\mathcal{N}_{o}
and DNNs
N_{\bm{\nu}}
,
\bm{\nu}\in\Gamma
, asserted by this lemma. For any
\bm{\nu}\in\Gamma
, we have
\bm{\nu}\in S
for some
S\in\mathcal{S}
, and any such
S
satisfies
|S|_{\bm{v}}\leq k
. Therefore
u^{2(5+\xi)}_{\bm{\nu}}=v^{2}_{\bm{\nu}}\leq k
for any
\bm{\nu}\in S
and we deduce that

{\|\bm{\nu}\|}_{1}\leq\prod^{d}_{j=1}(2\nu_{j}+1)=u^{2}_{\bm{\nu}}\leq k^{1/(5+\xi)}.

This implies that
m(\Gamma)\leq k^{1/(5+\xi)}
and therefore

\mathrm{width}(\mathcal{N}_{o})\lesssim k^{1/(5+\xi)},\quad\mathrm{depth}(\mathcal{N}_{o})\lesssim\log(k).

With this in hand, we now define the family
\mathcal{N}
of DNNs
N:\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}
by

\mathcal{N}=\left\{N=\bm{C}\begin{bmatrix}N_{\bm{\nu}_{1}}\\
\vdots\\
N_{\bm{\nu}_{|S|}}\\
0\\
\vdots\\
0\end{bmatrix}:S=\{\bm{\nu}_{1},\ldots,\bm{\nu}_{|S|}\}\in\mathcal{S},\ \bm{C}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}\times\lfloor k\rfloor}\right\}.

(D.19)

Here we also use the fact that
|S|\leq|S|_{\bm{\bm{v}}}\leq k
.
Notice that this family satisfies

\mathrm{width}(\mathcal{N})\leq k^{1+1/(5+\xi)},\quad\mathrm{depth}(\mathcal{N})\lesssim\log(k),

(D.20)

due to the bounds for
\mathcal{N}_{o}
.

Now let
N\in\mathcal{N}
and write
\bm{C}=[\bm{c}_{1}|\cdots|\bm{c}_{\lfloor k\rfloor}]
, where
\bm{c}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}
. Then

\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ N=\sum^{|S|}_{i=1}\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\bm{c}_{i})N_{\bm{\nu}_{i}}=\sum^{|S|}_{i=1}c_{i}N_{\bm{\nu}_{i}},\quad\text{where }c_{i}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}.

Therefore, we can associate
\mathcal{N}
with the space of functions
\widetilde{P}_{\mathcal{S};\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}}
, where, for any arbitrary Banach space
(\mathcal{Z},{\|\cdot\|}_{\mathcal{Z}})
,

\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathcal{S};\mathcal{Z}}=\left\{\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S}c_{\bm{\nu}}N_{\bm{\nu}}:c_{\bm{\nu}}\in\mathcal{Z},\ S\in\mathcal{S}\right\}.

We now require the following lemma, which relates the distance between a function in
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathcal{S};\mathcal{Z}}
and the corresponding polynomial in
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S};\mathcal{Z}}
.

Lemma D.10

(Discrete norms of polynomials and their approximating DNNs)
.

Let
S\subset\mathcal{F}
,
(\mathcal{Z},{\|\cdot\|}_{\mathcal{Z}})
be any Banach space and suppose that
p=\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S}c_{\bm{\nu}}\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}
, where
c_{\bm{\nu}}\in\mathcal{Z}
. Define

\tilde{p}=\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S}c_{\bm{\nu}}N_{\bm{\nu}}.

Then

{\|p-\tilde{p}\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}\leq{\|p-\tilde{p}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Z})}\leq\delta\sqrt{|S|}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Z})}.

(D.21)

Moreover, if
\mathcal{Z}=\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}
,
S\in\mathcal{S}
and (
D.7
) holds with
\alpha
satisfying
\delta\sqrt{|S|_{\bm{u}}}/\alpha<1
then

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\leq\frac{1}{\alpha-\delta\sqrt{|S|}}{\|\tilde{p}\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}.

Proof.

By (A.III) and the definition of the
N_{\bm{\nu}}
we have

\displaystyle{\|p-\tilde{p}\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}

\displaystyle\leq{\|p-\tilde{p}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Z})}

\displaystyle=\sup_{z^{*}\in B(\mathcal{Z}^{*})}{\|z^{*}(p-\tilde{p})\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D)}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{z^{*}\in B(\mathcal{Z}^{*})}\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S}|z^{*}(c_{\bm{\nu}})|{\|\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}-N_{\bm{\nu}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D)}

\displaystyle\leq\delta{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\bm{c}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{1;\mathcal{Z}}.

We now apply the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and Parseval’s identity to obtain

{\|p-\tilde{p}\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}\leq\delta\sqrt{|S|}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\bm{c}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2;\mathcal{Z}}=\delta\sqrt{|S|_{\bm{u}}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Z})},

which gives the first result.

For the second result, we apply (
D.7
) with
q=0
to get

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\leq\alpha^{-1}{\|p\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}\leq\alpha^{-1}\left({\|p-\tilde{p}\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}+{\|\tilde{p}\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}\right).

Using (
D.21
) and the fact that
\delta\sqrt{|S|}/\alpha<1
now gives the result.
∎

As seen in this section, a key step in our proofs is emulating the polynomials via DNNs of quantifiable width and depth. There is an extensive literature on this topic. See, e.g.,
[
25
,
38
,
63
,
57
,
67
,
97
,
85
,
5
,
23
,
72
,
79
,
92
,
21
,
78
,
86
]

and references therein. The proof of Lemma
D.9
reduces this to the task of emulating the multiplication operation
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\mapsto x_{1}\cdots x_{d}\in\mathbb{R}
via a DNN. As shown in the proof of
[
5
, Thm. 7.4]
(which is based on
[
79
, Prop. 2.6]
), this can in turn be achieved using a binary tree of
\lceil\log_{2}(d)\rceil
DNNs that approximately compute the multiplication of two numbers
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\mapsto xy\in\mathbb{R}
. Further, this task can be achieved via the identity
xy=((x+y)^{2}-(x-y)^{2})/4
by using a DNN that approximately computes the squaring function
x\in\mathbb{R}\mapsto x^{2}\in\mathbb{R}
. To summarize, provide a DNN of quantifiable width and depth can approximately compute the squaring function, it can also approximately emulate the multivariate Legendre polynomials.

In view of this, we can adapt our main theorems to various other activation functions without change. This includes Rectified Polynomial Units (RePUs), where the emulation is, in fact, exact (see, e.g.,
[
57
, Lem. 2.1]
). It also includes the Exponential Linear Unit (ELU) used in our numerical experiments and many others. See, for instance, Proposition 4.7 of
[
38
]
and the ensuing discussion. Rectified Linear Units (ReLUs) are slightly different, as in this case the depth of the DNN that performs the approximation multiplication depends on the desired accuracy (see, e.g.,
[
79
, Prop. 2.6]
). One could modify Theorem
3.1
to consider ReLU DNNs, with the result being a worse depth bound than that presented for tanh DNNs.

D.6
Analysis of (
2.5
)

Lemma D.12

(Approximate minimizers of (
2.5
) yield approximate minimizers of (
D.3
))
.

Suppose that (
D.7
) holds, let
\mathcal{N}
be the family of DNNs defined in §
D.5
and
\widehat{N}
be any
(\sigma,\tau)
-approximate minimizer of (
2.5
). Let

\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\widehat{N}=\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S}\hat{c}_{\bm{\nu}}N_{\bm{\nu}}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathcal{S};\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}},

where
S\in\mathcal{S}
and
\hat{c}_{\bm{\nu}}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}
, and define

\hat{p}=\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S}\hat{c}_{\bm{\nu}}\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}.

Then
\hat{p}
is a
(\sigma^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})
-approximate minimizer of (
D.3
), where

\begin{split}\sigma^{\prime}&\leq\sigma(1+\delta\sqrt{k}/\alpha)\\
\tau^{\prime}&\leq\tau+\sigma\delta\sqrt{k}/\alpha\left({\|F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}\right)+\delta\sqrt{k}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{p}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\end{split}.

(D.22)

Proof.

Let
p=\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S}{c}_{\bm{\nu}}\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}\in P_{\mathcal{S};\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}}
be arbitrary and
N\in\mathcal{N}
be the corresponding DNN so that
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ N=\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S}{c}_{\bm{\nu}}N_{\bm{\nu}}
. Then by the triangle inequality and the fact that
\widehat{N}
is an approximate minimizer, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|Y_{i}-\hat{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|Y_{i}-\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\widehat{N}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}+{\|\hat{p}-\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\widehat{N}\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}

\displaystyle\leq\sigma\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|Y_{i}-\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ N\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}+\tau+{\|\hat{p}-\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\widehat{N}\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}

\displaystyle\leq\sigma\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|Y_{i}-p\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}+\tau+\sigma{\|p-\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ N\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}+{\|\hat{p}-\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\widehat{N}\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}.

We now apply Lemma
D.10
to the last two terms, noting that
|S|\leq|S|_{\bm{v}}\leq k
since
S\in\mathcal{S}
, to obtain

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|Y_{i}-\hat{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}\leq

\displaystyle~\sigma\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|Y_{i}-p\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}

\displaystyle+\tau+\sigma\delta\sqrt{k}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}+\delta\sqrt{k}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{p}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}.

Consider the third term. We first apply (
D.7
) with
q=0
to get

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\leq\alpha^{-1}{\|p\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}=\alpha^{-1}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}.

We then use the triangle inequality to get

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|p\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}

\displaystyle\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-p\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|Y_{i}-p\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}+{\|F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}.

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|Y_{i}-\hat{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}\leq

\displaystyle~\sigma(1+\delta\sqrt{k}/\alpha)\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|Y_{i}-p\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}+\tau

\displaystyle+\sigma\delta\sqrt{k}/\alpha\left({\|F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}\right)+\delta\sqrt{k}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{p}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}.

Since
p\in P_{\mathcal{S};\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}}
was arbitrary we get the result.
∎

Theorem D.13

(Error bound for DNN minimizers)
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}:\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}=\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}})
be a bounded linear operator,
\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}={\|\mathcal{Q}\|}_{\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}}
and suppose that (
D.7
) holds with
\alpha\geq c_{0}
and
\alpha-\delta\sqrt{k}\geq c_{1}
for suitable universal constants
c_{0},c_{1}>0
. Let
\mathcal{N}
be the family of DNNs defined in §
D.5
and
\widehat{N}
be any
(\sigma,\tau)
-approximate minimizer of (
2.5
). Then the approximation
\widehat{F}=\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\widehat{N}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}
satisfies

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\widehat{F}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim

\displaystyle~{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sigma{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}

\displaystyle+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}\left({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sigma{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}\right)

\displaystyle+\tau+\sigma\delta\sqrt{k}{\|F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}

and

\displaystyle{\|F-\widehat{F}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{k}\sigma{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}

\displaystyle+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}\left({\|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{k}\sigma{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}\right)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{k}\tau+\sigma\delta k{\|F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\frac{\sqrt{k}\sigma}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}

for all
q\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S};\mathcal{Y}}
.

Proof.

Let
\hat{p}
be as in Lemma
D.12
. Then

\begin{split}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\widehat{F}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}&\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\hat{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}-\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\widehat{N}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\\
{\|F-\widehat{F}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}&\leq{\|F-\hat{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+{\|\hat{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}-\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\widehat{N}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\end{split}.

(D.23)

For the second term, we have, by (A.III) and Lemma
D.10
,

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}-\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\widehat{N}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}

\displaystyle\leq{\|\hat{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}-\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\widehat{N}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}

\displaystyle={\|\hat{p}-\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\widehat{N}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Y})}

\displaystyle\leq{\|\hat{p}-\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\widehat{N}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}

\displaystyle\leq\delta\sqrt{k}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{p}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}.

We now apply this, Lemma
D.12
, Theorem
D.6
and the facts that
\alpha^{-1}\lesssim 1
and
\sigma^{\prime}\geq 1
to obtain

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\widehat{F}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim

\displaystyle~{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sigma^{\prime}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}

\displaystyle+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}\left({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sigma^{\prime}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}\right)

\displaystyle+\tau^{\prime}+\frac{\sigma^{\prime}}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}

and

\displaystyle{\|F-\widehat{F}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{k}\sigma^{\prime}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}

\displaystyle+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}\left({\|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{k}\sigma^{\prime}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}\right)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{k}\tau^{\prime}+\frac{\sqrt{k}\sigma^{\prime}}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}

for any
q\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S};\mathcal{Y}}
, where
\sigma^{\prime}
and
\tau^{\prime}
are as in (
D.22
). Due to the various assumptions, these satisfy

\sigma^{\prime}\lesssim\sigma,\quad\tau^{\prime}\lesssim\tau+\sigma\delta\sqrt{k}{\|F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}+\delta\sqrt{k}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{p}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}.

We deduce that

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\widehat{F}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim

\displaystyle~{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sigma{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}

\displaystyle+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}\left({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sigma{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}\right)

\displaystyle+\tau+\sigma\delta\sqrt{k}{\|F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}+\delta\sqrt{k}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{p}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}

and

\displaystyle{\|F-\widehat{F}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{k}\sigma{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}

\displaystyle+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}\left({\|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{k}\sigma{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}\right)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{k}\tau+\sigma\delta k{\|F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\frac{\sqrt{k}\sigma}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}+\delta k{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{p}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}.

We now bound the final term. Using Lemma
D.10
once more, in combination with the fact that
\alpha-\delta\sqrt{k}\gtrsim 1
, we see that

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{p}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\leq\frac{1}{\alpha-\delta\sqrt{k}}{\|\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\widehat{N}\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\|\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\widehat{N}\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}.

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|Y_{i}-\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\widehat{N}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{Y}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}

\displaystyle\leq\sigma\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|Y_{i}-0\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}+\tau+\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{Y}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\sigma)\left({\|F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}\right)+\tau.

Here, we also used the fact that
\widehat{N}
is an approximate minimizer in the fourth step, as well as the facts that the zero network
0\in\mathcal{N}
and that
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}
is a linear map. Plugging this into the previous expressions now gives the result.
∎

D.7
Bounding the best polynomial approximation error terms

When
\sigma=1
(as will be the case when we come to prove Theorem
3.1
), the error bounds in Theorem
D.13
involve best polynomial approximation error terms of the form

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu},\quad{\|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{k}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}

for arbitrary
q\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S};\mathcal{Y}}
. By the triangle inequality, these are bounded by

\begin{split}E_{2}(F,q):=&~{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\iota|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\iota|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}\\
&+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|q\circ\iota-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|q\circ\iota-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu},\\
E_{\infty}(F,q):=&~{\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{k}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\iota|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}\\
&+{\|q\circ\iota-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{k}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|q\circ\iota-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}.\end{split}

(D.24)

In this section, we construct a suitable polynomial
q
in the case where (A.II) holds and thereby derive a bound for these term.
We first require the following lemma.

Lemma D.14
.

Let
G\in L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})
and
0<\epsilon<1
. Then the following hold.

(a)

With probability at least
1-\epsilon
on the draw of the
X_{i}
, we have

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|G|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}\leq{\|G\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}/\sqrt{\epsilon}.

(b)

Suppose that
m\geq 2r\log(2/\epsilon)
for some
r>0
. Then, with probability at least
1-\epsilon
on the draw of the
X_{i}
, we have

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|G|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}\leq\sqrt{2}\left({\|G\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}/\sqrt{r}+{\|G\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\right).

Proof.

Observe that the random variable
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|G|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}^{2}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}
satisfies

\mathbb{E}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|G|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}^{2}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}={\|G\|}^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}.

For (a), we use Markov’s inequality to get

\mathbb{P}\left({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|G|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}\geq{\|G\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}/\sqrt{\epsilon}\right)\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|G|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}^{2}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}}{{\|G\|}^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}/\epsilon}=\epsilon,

as required.

The proof of (b) is based on
[
3
, Lem. 7.11]
. We repeat it here for convenience.
Define the random variable
Z_{i}={\|G(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}
and observe that

\mathbb{E}(Z_{i})=\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mu}{\|G(X)\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}={\|G\|}^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}=:a.

Let
X_{i}=Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}(Z_{i})
so that

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|G|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}^{2}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}=\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}Z_{i}=\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}X_{i}+a.

Now let
b={\|G\|}^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}
and observe that

X_{i}\leq Z_{i}\leq b,\quad-X_{i}\leq\mathbb{E}(Z_{i})\leq b,\quad\text{a.s..}

We also have

\sum^{m}_{i=1}\mathbb{E}(X^{2}_{i})\leq\sum^{m}_{i=1}\mathbb{E}(Z^{2}_{i})\leq b\sum^{m}_{i=1}\mathbb{E}(Z_{i})=abm.

Therefore, Bernstein’s inequality for bounded random variables (see, e.g.,
[
29
, Cor. 7.31]
) implies that

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}X_{i}\right|\geq t\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}m/2}{ab+bt/3}\right)

for any
t>0.
We now set
t=a+b/r
and notice that
\frac{t^{2}m/2}{ab+bt/3}\geq\frac{3m}{5r}\geq\log(2/\epsilon)
. Therefore,

\left|\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}X_{i}\right|<a+b/r

with probability at least
1-\epsilon
. It follows that

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|G|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}\leq\sqrt{2a+b/r}\leq\sqrt{2}\left(\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b/r}\right)
