with the same probability. Substituting the values for
a
and
b
now gives the result.
∎

Lemma D.15
.

Let
F\in L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})
,
q\in\mathcal{P}_{S;\mathcal{Y}}
be arbitrary and
m\geq 2r\log(6/\epsilon)
for some
r>0
and
0<\epsilon<1
. Then

\displaystyle E_{2}(F,q)\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}/\sqrt{r}+{\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{k/\epsilon}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|q|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}{\|\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}-\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{X}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}))}.

and

\displaystyle E_{\infty}(F,q)\lesssim

\displaystyle(1+\sqrt{k/r}){\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{k}{\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}

\displaystyle+k{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|q|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}{\|\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}-\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{X}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}))}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{k}(1+\sqrt{k/r}){|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|q|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}{\|\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}-\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{X}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}))}

with probability at least
1-\epsilon
.

Proof.

We apply part (b) of Lemma
D.14
with
\epsilon
replaced by
\epsilon/3
to the term
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\iota|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}
and parts (a) and (b) of Lemma
D.14
with
\epsilon
replaced by
\epsilon/3
to the term
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|q\circ\iota-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}
. Using the union bound, this gives that

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\iota|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+{\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}/\sqrt{r}

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|q\circ\iota-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\|q\circ\iota-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}/\sqrt{\epsilon}

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|q\circ\iota-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\|q\circ\iota-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}//\sqrt{r}+{\|q\circ\iota-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}

with probability at least
1-\epsilon
.
This yields

\displaystyle E_{2}(F,q)\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+{\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}/\sqrt{r}

\displaystyle+{\|q\circ\iota-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}/\sqrt{\epsilon}

\displaystyle E_{\infty}(F,q)\lesssim

\displaystyle~(1+\sqrt{k/r}){\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{k}{\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}

\displaystyle+(1+\sqrt{k/r}){\|q\circ\iota-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{k}{\|q\circ\iota-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}

with the same probability.
It remains to bound the terms involving
q\circ\iota-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}
.
Using (A.III), Lemma
D.2
and the fact that
q
depends on its first
d_{\mathcal{X}}
variables only, we see that

{\|q\circ\iota(X)-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X)\|}_{\mathcal{Y}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{k}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|q|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}{\|\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}(X)-\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X)\|}_{\infty}.

Therefore

\displaystyle{\|q\circ\iota-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim\sqrt{k}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|q|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}{\|\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}-\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{N}))}

\displaystyle{\|q\circ\iota-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim\sqrt{k}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|q|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}{\|\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}-\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{N}))}

Substituting this into the previous bounds and using the definition of
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}
from (A.III) now gives the result.
∎

We are now ready to choose the polynomial
q
. First, we require the following technical lemma, which shows the existence of multi-index sets of weighted cardinality
k
which achieve the desired algebraic rates of convergence.

Lemma D.16
.

Let
f=\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{F}}c_{\bm{\nu}}\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}
satisfy
f\in\mathcal{H}(\bm{b})
for some
\bm{b}\in\ell^{p}(\mathbb{N})
with
\bm{b}\geq\bm{0}
. Then there are index sets
S_{1},S_{2}\subset\mathcal{F}
with
|S_{1}|_{\bm{v}},|S_{2}|_{\bm{v}}\leq k
such that

{\|\bm{c}-\bm{c}_{S_{1}}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}\leq C(\bm{b},p,\xi)\cdot k^{1/2-1/p},\quad{\|\bm{c}-\bm{c}_{S_{2}}\|}_{1,\bm{v};\mathcal{Y}}\leq C(\bm{b},p,\xi)\cdot k^{1-1/p},

where
C(\bm{b},p,\xi)\geq 0
depends on
\bm{b}
,
p
and
\xi
only.

Proof.

We first show that
\bm{c}\in\ell^{p}_{\bm{v}}(\mathcal{F};\mathcal{Y})
. By definition of
\mathcal{H}(\bm{b})
(see (
2.3
)),
f
is holomorphic in every Bernstein polyellipse
\mathcal{E}(\bm{\rho})
for which
\bm{\rho}
satisfies

\bm{\rho}\geq\bm{1},\quad\sum^{\infty}_{j=1}\left(\frac{\rho_{j}+\rho^{-1}_{j}}{2}-1\right)b_{j}\leq 1.

(D.25)

Using
[
4
, Lem. 5.3]
(which is based on
[
102
, Cor. B.2.7]
) with
\bm{\alpha}=\bm{\beta}=\bm{0}
, we get that
{\|c_{\bm{0}}\|}_{\mathcal{Y}}\leq 1
and

{\|c_{\bm{\nu}}\|}_{\mathcal{Y}}\leq\prod_{k\in I(\bm{\nu},\bm{\rho})}\frac{\rho^{-\nu_{k}+1}_{k}}{(\rho_{k}-1)^{2}}(\nu_{k}+1),\quad\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{F}\backslash\{\bm{0}\}

for all such
\bm{\rho}
, where
I(\bm{\nu},\bm{\rho})=\mathrm{supp}(\bm{\nu})\cap\{k:\rho_{k}>1\}
.
Define the sequence
d_{\bm{0}}=1
and

d_{\bm{\nu}}=v^{2/p-1}_{\bm{\nu}}\cdot\inf\left\{\prod_{k\in I(\bm{\nu},\bm{\rho})}\frac{\rho^{-\nu_{k}+1}_{k}}{(\rho_{k}-1)^{2}}(\nu_{k}+1):\text{$\bm{\rho}$ satisfies \eqref{rho-admissible}}\right\},\quad\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{F}\backslash\{\bm{0}\}.

Using (
C.3
)-(
C.4
), we see that

v_{\bm{\nu}}=\prod_{k\in\mathrm{supp}(\bm{\nu})}(2\nu_{k}+1)^{(5+\xi)/2}

and therefore

d_{\bm{\nu}}\leq\prod_{k\in I(\bm{\nu},\bm{\rho})}\frac{\rho^{-\nu_{k}+1}_{k}}{(\rho_{k}-1)^{2}}(2\nu_{k}+1)^{\gamma}

for all
\bm{\rho}
satisfying (
D.25
) and some
\gamma=\gamma(p,\xi)\geq 0
. We now use
[
4
, Lem. 5.4]
to deduce that
\bm{d}=(d_{\bm{\nu}})_{\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{F}}\in\ell^{p}(\mathbb{N})
with
{\|\bm{d}\|}_{p}\leq C(\bm{b},p,\xi)
for some
C(\bm{b},p,\xi)\geq 0
depending on
\bm{b}
,
p
and
\xi
only. Returning to
\bm{c}
, this gives

{\|\bm{c}\|}^{p}_{p,\bm{v};\mathcal{Y}}=\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{F}}v^{2-p}_{\bm{\nu}}{\|c_{\bm{\nu}}\|}^{p}_{\mathcal{Y}}\leq\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{F}}|d_{\bm{\nu}}|^{p}\leq C(\bm{b},p,\xi)^{p}.

Hence
\bm{c}\in\ell^{p}_{\bm{v}}(\mathcal{F};\mathcal{Y})
with
{\|\bm{c}\|}_{p,\bm{v};\mathcal{Y}}\leq C(\bm{b},p,\xi)
, as required.

The second step involves the application of the weighted Stechkin’s inequality (see
[
3
, Lem. 3.12]
). This gives that

\min\left\{{\|\bm{c}-\bm{c}_{S}\|}_{q,\bm{v};\mathcal{Y}}:S\subset\mathcal{F},\ |S|_{\bm{v}}\leq k\right\}=:\sigma_{k}(\bm{c})_{q,\bm{v};\mathcal{Y}}\leq{\|\bm{c}\|}_{p,\bm{v};\mathcal{Y}}k^{1/q-1/p},

for any
q\in(p,2]
and
k>0
. Applying this result with
q=2
implies the existence of the set
S_{1}
(recall that
{\|\cdot\|}_{2,\bm{v};\mathcal{Y}}={\|\cdot\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}
) and applying it with
q=1
implies the existence of the set
S_{2}
.
∎

We now define the set

\Lambda^{\mathsf{HCI}}_{n}=\left\{\bm{\nu}=(\nu_{k})^{\infty}_{k=1}\in\mathcal{F}:\prod_{k:\nu_{k}\neq 0}(\nu_{k}+1)\leq n,\ \nu_{k}=0,\ k>n\right\}\subset\mathcal{F}.

(D.26)

Notice that
\Lambda^{\mathsf{HCI}}_{n}
is isomorphic to an index set in
\mathbb{N}^{n}_{0}
by the natural restriction map.

Lemma D.17
.

Let
k>0
and suppose that
\Lambda\supseteq\Lambda^{\mathsf{HCI}}_{n}
for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
f=\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{F}}c_{\bm{\nu}}\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}
satisfy
f\in\mathcal{H}(\bm{b})
for some
\bm{b}\in\ell^{p}_{\mathsf{M}}(\mathbb{N})
with
\bm{b}\geq\bm{0}
. Then there exists an index set
S\in\mathcal{S}
such that

{\|f-f_{S}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\leq C(\bm{b},p,\xi)\cdot\left(k^{1-1/p}+n^{1-1/p}\right),

(D.27)

where
f_{S}=\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S}c_{\bm{\nu}}\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}
.
Moreover, if
\mathcal{Y}
is a Hilbert space, then we also have that

{\|f-f_{S}\|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\leq C(\bm{b},p,\xi)\cdot\left(k^{1/2-1/p}+n^{1/2-1/p}\right).

(D.28)

Here
C(\bm{b},p,\xi)>0
is a constant depending on
\bm{b}
,
p
and
\xi
only.

Proof of Lemma
D.17
.

The previous lemma implies that there exist index sets
S_{1},S_{2}\subset\mathcal{F}
with
|S_{1}|_{\bm{v}},|S_{2}|_{\bm{v}}\leq k/2
such that

{\|\bm{c}-\bm{c}_{S_{1}}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}\leq C(\bm{b},p,\xi)\cdot k^{1/2-1/p},\quad{\|\bm{c}-\bm{c}_{S_{2}}\|}_{1,\bm{v};\mathcal{Y}}\leq C(\bm{b},p,\xi)\cdot k^{1-1/p}.

Now define
S=S_{1}\cup S_{2}\cap\Lambda
and notice that
S\subseteq\Lambda
and
|S|_{\bm{v}}\leq|S_{1}|_{\bm{v}}+|S_{2}|_{\bm{v}}\leq k
. Hence
S\in\mathcal{S}
. Since
v_{\bm{\nu}}\geq u_{\bm{\nu}}={\|\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D)}
, we have (using
[
5
, Lem. 5.1]
)

{\|f-f_{S}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\leq{\|\bm{c}-\bm{c}_{S}\|}_{1,\bm{u};\mathcal{Y}}\leq{\|\bm{c}-\bm{c}_{S_{2}}\|}_{1,\bm{v};\mathcal{Y}}+{\|\bm{c}-\bm{c}_{\Lambda}\|}_{1,\bm{u};\mathcal{Y}}.

Hence, to complete the proof of the first result, we need only show that

{\|\bm{c}-\bm{c}_{\Lambda}\|}_{1,\bm{u};\mathcal{Y}}\leq C(\bm{b},p)\cdot n^{1-1/p}

for some constant
C(\bm{b},p)\geq 0
.
First, by construction,
\Lambda\supseteq\Lambda^{\mathsf{HCI}}_{n}
contains every anchored set of size at most
n
. See, e.g.. Therefore
{\|\bm{c}-\bm{c}_{\Lambda}\|}_{1,\bm{u};\mathcal{Y}}\leq{\|\bm{c}-\bm{c}_{S}\|}_{1,\bm{u};\mathcal{Y}}
for any such set
S
. The result now follows from
[
5
, Cor. 8.2]
.

Now suppose that
\mathcal{Y}
is a Hilbert space. Then Parseval’s identity gives that

{\|f-f_{S}\|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}={\|\bm{c}-\bm{c}_{S}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}\leq{\|\bm{c}-\bm{c}_{S_{1}}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}+{\|\bm{c}-\bm{c}_{\Lambda}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}.

As before, it suffices to show that

{\|\bm{c}-\bm{c}_{\Lambda}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}\leq C(\bm{b},p)\cdot n^{1/2-1/p}.

This follows from the same approach and
[
5
, Cor. 8.2]
once more.
∎

D.8
Final arguments

We are now, finally, ready to prove Theorem
3.1
. We first consider the case where
\mathcal{Y}
is a Banach space, then treat the case where
\mathcal{Y}
is a Hilbert space afterwards.

Proof of Theorem
3.1
when
\mathcal{Y}
is a Banach space.

We divide the proof into a series of steps.

Step 1: Setup and DNN width/depth bounds.
Let
m
,
\delta
,
\epsilon
and
L
be as in the theorem statement. We may without loss of generality assume that
\delta\leq 1/5
. Now let

n=\left\lceil\frac{m}{L}\right\rceil\leq d_{\mathcal{X}}

(D.29)

and

\Lambda=\Lambda^{\mathsf{HCI}}_{n},

where
\Lambda^{\mathsf{HCI}}_{n}
is as in (
D.26
). We also set

\xi=1/\delta-5\geq 0

(D.30)

and

k=\frac{m}{cL},

(D.31)

where
c\geq 1
is a universal constant that will be chosen in the next step. Finally, let
\delta=2^{-m}/m
and
\mathcal{N}
by the tanh DNN family (
D.19
), where the
N_{\bm{\nu}}
are as in Lemma
D.9
for this
\Lambda
and value of
\delta
.

By (
D.20
) and the definition of
\xi
and
k
, we immediately see that

\mathrm{width}(\mathcal{N})\lesssim(m/L)^{1+\delta},\qquad\mathrm{depth}(\mathcal{N})\lesssim\log(m/L).

This yields the width and depth bounds (
3.1
). The rest of the proof is therefore devoted to showing the error bounds (
3.3
)-(
3.4
).

Step 2: Ensuring (
D.7
) holds with probability at least
1-\epsilon/2
.
A standard bound (see, e.g., the proof of Lemma 6.4 in
[
5
]
) gives that
N=|\Lambda|
satisfies

\log(\mathrm{e}N)\leq 4\log^{2}(\mathrm{e}n)\lesssim\log^{2}(m).

(D.32)

Here, in the final step we used the fact that
m\geq 3
and
L(m,\epsilon)\geq 1
. This and the fact that
c\geq 1
also implies that
k\leq m
. Therefore, the right-hand side of (
D.13
) with
\epsilon
replaced by
\epsilon/2
satisfies

c_{0}\cdot k\cdot(\log(\mathrm{e}N)\cdot\log^{2}(k)+\log(4/\epsilon))\lesssim k\cdot L(m,\epsilon).

(D.33)

Hence, for sufficiently large
c\geq 1
, we deduce that (
D.13
) holds with
\epsilon/2
. Using (A.I), we see that

\begin{split}{\|\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}-\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{X}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{q}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{N}))}\leq L_{\iota}{\|\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{X}}-\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{X}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{q}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{X})},\quad q=2,\infty.\end{split}

(D.34)

Hence (
D.14
) is implied by (
3.2
). We conclude from Lemma
D.8
that (
D.7
) holds with probability at least
1-\epsilon/2
and
\alpha\gtrsim 1
.

Step 3: Error analysis.
Let
f\in\mathcal{H}(\bm{b})
be the function asserted by (A.II) and define
q=f_{S}
as the polynomial asserted by Lemma
D.17
with
n
as in (
D.29
). We also observe that

{\|F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}={\|f\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varsigma}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim{\|f\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim 1,

(D.35)

since
f\in\mathcal{H}(\bm{b})
. We now apply Theorem
D.13
with
\sigma=1
and use (
D.31
), the definition of
\delta
and (
3.8
) to see that

\begin{split}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\widehat{F}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim&~{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}\\
&+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}E_{2}(F,q)+E_{\mathsf{opt},2}+E_{\mathsf{samp},2}\\
{\|F-\widehat{F}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim&~{\|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{k}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}\\
&+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}E_{\infty}(F,q)+E_{\mathsf{opt},\infty}+E_{\mathsf{samp},\infty}\end{split}

(D.36)

with probability at least
1-\epsilon/2
, where
E_{2}(F,q)
and
E_{\infty}(F,q)
are as in (
D.24
),
\mathcal{Q}:\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}=\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}})
is any bounded linear operator and
\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}={\|\mathcal{Q}\|}_{\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}}
.

Notice that Parseval’s identity and (
D.35
) imply that

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|q|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\leq{\|f\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim 1.

Hence, this, the previous bounds, Lemma
D.15
with
\epsilon
replaced by
\epsilon/4
and the union bound yield

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\widehat{F}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim

\displaystyle~{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}

\displaystyle+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}\left({\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}/\sqrt{r}+{\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\right)

\displaystyle+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}\sqrt{k/\epsilon}{\|\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}-\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{X}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}))}

\displaystyle+E_{\mathsf{opt},2}+E_{\mathsf{samp},2}

and

\displaystyle{\|F-\widehat{F}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{k}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}

\displaystyle+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}\left((1+\sqrt{k/r}){\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{k}{\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\right)

\displaystyle+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}k{\|\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}-\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{X}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}))}

\displaystyle+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}\sqrt{k}(1+\sqrt{k/r}){\|\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}-\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{X}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}))}

\displaystyle+E_{\mathsf{opt},\infty}+E_{\mathsf{samp},\infty}

with probability at least
1-3\epsilon/4
, for any
r
such that
m\geq 2r\log(24/\epsilon)
. In particular, we may choose
r=k
due the definition of
k
(
D.31
). We next bound the discrete error
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}
. Applying Lemma
D.14
with
\epsilon
replaced by
\epsilon/8
and the union bound, we see that

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}/\sqrt{\epsilon}

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}/\sqrt{r}+{\|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}

with probability at least
1-\epsilon/4
, provided
m\geq 2r\log(16/\epsilon)
. In particular, we may take
r=k
once more. Substituting this into the previous expressions, setting
r=k
throughout, using the union bound once more and recalling (
3.7
), (
D.31
) and (
D.34
), we deduce that

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\widehat{F}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}/\sqrt{\epsilon}

\displaystyle+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}\left({\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}/\sqrt{m/L}+{\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\right)

\displaystyle+(\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}/a_{\mathcal{Y}})\cdot E_{\mathcal{X},2}+E_{\mathsf{opt},2}+E_{\mathsf{samp},2}

and

\displaystyle{\|F-\widehat{F}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{m/L}{\|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}

\displaystyle+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}\left({\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{m/L}{\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\right)

\displaystyle+(\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}/a_{\mathcal{Y}})\cdot E_{\mathcal{X},\infty}+E_{\mathsf{opt},\infty}+E_{\mathsf{samp},\infty}

with probability at least
1-\epsilon
. This holds for any bounded linear operator
\mathcal{Q}:\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}=D_{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}})
. We now set
\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{Y}}
, which is linear and bounded by (A.IV) with
\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}={\|\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{Y}}\|}_{\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}}=a_{\mathcal{Y}}
by definition.
Using this, we observe that

{\|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F\|}_{L^{q}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}={\|\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{Y}}-\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{Y}}\|}_{L^{q}_{F\sharp\mu}(\mathcal{Y};\mathcal{Y})},\quad q=2,\infty.

Substituting this into the previous expressions and recalling (
3.7
) now gives

\begin{split}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\widehat{F}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim&~a_{\mathcal{Y}}\left({\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}/\sqrt{m/L}+{\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\right)\\
&+E_{\mathcal{X},2}+E_{\mathcal{Y},2}+E_{\mathsf{opt},2}+E_{\mathsf{samp},2}\\
{\|F-\widehat{F}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim&~a_{\mathcal{Y}}\left({\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{m/L}{\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\right)\\
&+E_{\mathcal{X},\infty}+E_{\mathcal{Y},\infty}+E_{\mathsf{opt},\infty}+E_{\mathsf{samp},\infty}.\end{split}

(D.37)

Step 4: Bounding the polynomial error terms.
It remains to bound the error terms
F-q\circ\iota
in (
D.37
).
Using (A.I), (A.II) and Lemma
D.17
, we now notice that

{\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}={\|f-q\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varsigma}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim{\|f-q\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim C(\bm{b},p,\xi)\cdot(k^{1-1/p}+n^{1-1/p}).

Recall that
n\geq k
. Therefore, using this, (
D.31
) and (
3.6
), we see that

\displaystyle a_{\mathcal{Y}}\left({\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}/\sqrt{m/L}+{\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim a_{\mathcal{Y}}{\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}

\displaystyle\lesssim a_{\mathcal{Y}}\cdot C(\bm{b},p,\xi)\cdot(m/L)^{1-1/p}

\displaystyle=E_{\mathsf{app},2}

and likewise for the
L^{\infty}_{\mu}
-norm bound. Combining this with (
D.37
) now completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.1
when
\mathcal{Y}
is a Hilbert space.

The two differences in theorem statement when
\mathcal{Y}
is a Hilbert space are: (i) the
L^{2}
-norm error is with respect to the stronger Bochner norm, and (ii) the approximation error terms
E_{\mathsf{app},q}
,
q=2,\infty
, are smaller by a factor of
1/2
in the exponent (recall (
3.6
)). We treat both issues separately.

For the (i), we commence with the supporting results in §
D.2
. First, we note that Lemmas
D.1
and
D.2
also hold in the Bochner
L^{2}
-norm, since these results already give upper bounds involving the Pettis
L^{2}
-norm. Next, we observe that the proof of Lemma
D.3
is readily adapted to yield an equivalent result in the Bochner
L^{2}
-norm with the same constant
\delta
. The same therefore applies to Lemma
D.4
.

We next consider the analysis of (
D.3
) in §
D.3
. If we replace (
D.7
) by the condition

{\|p-q\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}\geq\alpha\max\{{\|p-q\|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})},{\|p-q\|}_{L^{2}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\},\quad\forall p,q\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S};\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}}

(D.38)

then the proof of Lemma
D.5
yields the same error bounds, except with the Pettis
L^{2}
-norm replaced by the Bochner
L^{2}
-norm. Theorem
D.6
is likewise modified to provide a bound in the Bochner
L^{2}
-norm.

Up to this point, we have not used the fact that
\mathcal{Y}
is a Hilbert space. We now need this property. As in §
D.4
, the next step is to establish that (
D.38
) holds with high probability. Lemma
D.7
is unchanged, therefore our focus is on Lemma
D.8
. We now describe the steps needed to modify the proof of this lemma to assert (
D.38
) subject to the same conditions (
D.13
)-(
D.14
). First, let
\{\varphi_{i}\}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}_{i=1}
be an orthonormal basis of
\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}
and write each coefficient
c_{\bm{\nu}_{i}}
of the function
h
in (
D.16
) as
c_{\bm{\nu}_{i}}=\sum^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}_{j=1}b_{ij}\varphi_{j}
for scalars
b_{ij}
. Then it is a short exercise to write

{\|h\|}^{2}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}={\|\bm{A}\bm{c}\|}^{2}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}=\sum^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}_{j=1}{\|\bm{A}\bm{b}_{j}\|}^{2}_{2},

where
\bm{b}_{j}=(b_{ij})^{N}_{i=1}
. Since
b_{ij}=0
,
\forall j
, whenever
c_{\bm{\nu}_{i}}=0
, this vector also satisfies
{\|\bm{b}_{j}\|}_{0,\bm{v}}\leq 2k
. Hence, by Lemma
D.7
and Parseval’s identity twice,

\displaystyle{\|h\|}^{2}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}

\displaystyle\geq\sum^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}_{j=1}(\theta_{-}-(1+\theta_{+}c_{5}\delta){\|\bm{b}_{j}\|}^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle=(\theta_{-}-(1+\theta_{+}c_{5}\delta)\sum^{N}_{i=1}{\|c_{\bm{\nu}_{i}}\|}^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle=(\theta_{-}-(1+\theta_{+}c_{5}\delta){\|h\|}^{2}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}.

We now use the bounds (
D.15
) (which are unchanged) and set
\delta=c_{3}/(2(1+c_{4})c_{5})
once more to get

{\|h\|}^{2}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}\geq c_{3}/2{\|h\|}^{2}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}.

This gives the desired result.

This completes the changes needed in order to analyze the polynomial training problem (
D.3
). We next consider the DNN training problem (
2.5
). §
D.5
remains unchanged. After reviewing their proofs, we see that Lemma
D.12
and Theorem
D.13
both hold with Pettis norms replaced by Bochner norms whenever (
D.38
) holds instead of (
D.7
). Finally, we also observe that Lemma
D.15
also holds with Pettis norms replaced by Bochner norms, both in the bound and in the definition (
D.24
) of
E_{2}(F,q)
and
E_{\infty}(F,q)
.

Having completed the changes needed in all the preparatory results, we now follow the same steps as above in the Banach space case. Steps 1 and 2 are unchanged. For Step 3, we go through and replace Pettis norms by Bochner norms throughout. Using this, we obtain (
D.37
), except with the Bochner norm on the left-hand side in the first inequality, i.e.,

\begin{split}{\|F-\widehat{F}\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim&~a_{\mathcal{Y}}\left({\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}/\sqrt{m/L}+{\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\right)\\
&+E_{\mathcal{X},2}+E_{\mathcal{Y},2}+E_{\mathsf{opt},2}+E_{\mathsf{samp},2}\\
{\|F-\widehat{F}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim&~a_{\mathcal{Y}}\left({\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{m/L}{\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\right)\\
&+E_{\mathcal{X},\infty}+E_{\mathcal{Y},\infty}+E_{\mathsf{opt},\infty}+E_{\mathsf{samp},\infty}.\end{split}

(D.39)

This concludes the changes needed to address (i). To address (ii), we bound the terms
F-q\circ\iota
. Using (A.I), (A.II), Lemma
D.17
, the fact that
\mathcal{Y}
is a Hilbert space and the definitions (
D.31
) and (
D.29
) of
k
and
n
, we see that

{\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}={\|f-q\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varsigma}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim{\|f-q\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim C(\bm{b},p,\xi)\cdot(m/L)^{1-1/p}

and

{\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}={\|f-q\|}_{L^{2}_{\varsigma}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim{\|f-q\|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim C(\bm{b},p,\xi)\cdot(m/L)^{1/2-1/p}

Substituting this into (
D.39
) and recalling (
3.6
) now completes the proof.
∎

Having seen the proof, we now summarize these differences as follows. First, the matter of whether the Pettis versus Bochner norm can be used reduces to the choice of such norm in the discrete metric inequality (
D.7
). When
\mathcal{Y}
is a Banach space, we are able to establish this in terms of the Pettis norm subject to a log-linear scaling between
m
and
k
(see Lemma
D.8
and (
D.13
)). However, when
\mathcal{Y}
is also a Hilbert space, we can establish the stronger version (
D.38
) of this inequality by exploiting the additional structure. This, in short, is what leads to the stronger norm bound in this case.

Second, in the Hilbert space case, we get an improved approximation error. This stems from (
D.17
) and, specifically, the fact that when
\mathcal{Y}
is a Hilbert space we may use Parseval’s identity in the Bochner space
L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})
to bound the
L^{2}_{\varrho}
-norm error term via (
D.28
). This is not possible when
\mathcal{Y}
is a Banach space, so we settle for bounding this term via (
D.27
) instead.

Appendix E
Proof of Theorem
3.2

E.1
Setup

As in §
D.1
, let
\Lambda\subset\mathcal{F}
with
\mathrm{supp}(\bm{\nu})\subseteq\{1,\ldots,d_{\mathcal{X}}\}
,
\forall\bm{\nu}\in\Lambda
, and write
N=|\Lambda|
. Let
\mathcal{S}
be as in (
D.1
),
r\in\mathbb{N}
,
r>\max\{m,k\}
(its precise value will be chosen later in the proof) and define the set

\Gamma=\Gamma_{o}\cup\bigcup_{S\in\mathcal{S}}S\subset\mathcal{F},\quad\text{where }\Gamma_{o}=\{\bm{e}_{i}:i=1,\ldots,r\}.

(E.1)

Finally, let
0<\delta<1
and consider the family
\mathcal{N}_{o}
and the tanh DNNs
\{N_{\bm{\nu}}\}_{\bm{\nu}\in\Gamma}
whose existence is implied by Lemma
D.9
. We will specify
\Lambda
,
k
,
r
and
\delta
later in the proof.

Next, let

\hat{p}=\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S}\hat{c}_{\bm{\nu}}\Psi_{\bm{\nu}}

be any minimizer of (
D.3
), where
|S|_{\bm{v}}\leq k
and define

\tilde{p}=\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S}\hat{c}_{\bm{\nu}}N_{\bm{\nu}}.

Let

\bm{B}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{r}}\left(N_{\bm{e}_{j}}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})\right)^{m,r}_{i,j=1}.

Now,
\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}:=\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}})
is a finite-dimensional subspace of the Banach space
\mathcal{Y}
. Hence, for any
Y\in\mathcal{Y}
there exists a closest point
\widetilde{Y}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}
, i.e., a point satisfying

{\|Y-\widetilde{Y}\|}_{\mathcal{Y}}=\inf\left\{{\|Y-Z\|}_{\mathcal{Y}}:Z\in\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}\right\}.

Given
Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m}
, let
\widetilde{Y}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}_{m}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}
be the corresponding closest points. Now define

\bm{e}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{r}}\left(\widetilde{Y}_{i}-\tilde{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})\right)^{m}_{i=1}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}^{m}

and

\bar{p}=\tilde{p}+\sum^{r}_{i=1}(\bm{B}^{{\dagger}}\bm{e}+y\bm{z})_{i}N_{\bm{e}_{i}},

(E.2)

where
\bm{z}\in N(\bm{B})\backslash\{\bm{0}\}
and
y\in\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}
,
{\|y\|}_{\mathcal{Y}}=1
, are arbitrary. Note that such a
\bm{z}
exists, since
r>m
by assumption.
Notice that

\bar{p}=\sum_{\bm{\nu}\in S\cup\Gamma_{o}}\bar{c}_{\bm{\nu}}N_{\bm{\nu}}

for coefficients
\bar{c}_{\bm{\nu}}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}
. Therefore, we can write

\bar{p}=\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\widehat{N},

(E.3)

where

\widehat{N}=\bm{C}\begin{bmatrix}N_{\bm{\nu}_{1}}\\
\vdots\\
N_{\bm{\nu}_{|S\cup\Gamma_{o}|}}\\
0\\
\vdots\\
0\end{bmatrix}

(E.4)

and
\bm{C}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}\times(\lfloor k\rfloor+r)}
. Finally, we define the approximation

\widehat{F}=\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\widehat{N}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}

(E.5)

and, for convenience,

{\mathchoice{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\displaystyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\textstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.40926pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.40926pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.78334pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=6.14795pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.9537pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.9537pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=3.41667pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=5.20493pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}}=\hat{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}.

E.2
Estimation of the DNN minimizer

Lemma E.1

(
\widehat{N}
is a minimizer)
.

If
\bm{B}
is full rank, then

\widehat{F}(X_{i})=\widetilde{Y}_{i},\quad\forall i=1,\ldots,m.

Therefore,
\widehat{N}
is a minimizer of (
2.5
).

Proof.

Observe that

\displaystyle\widehat{F}(X_{i})

\displaystyle=\overline{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})

\displaystyle=\tilde{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})+\sqrt{r}\sum^{r}_{i=1}(\bm{B})_{ij}(\bm{B}^{{\dagger}}\bm{e}+y\bm{z})_{j}

\displaystyle=\tilde{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})+\sqrt{r}(\bm{B}(\bm{B}^{{\dagger}}\bm{e}+y\bm{z}))_{i}

\displaystyle=\tilde{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})+\sqrt{r}(\bm{e})_{i}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{Y}_{i}.

Here, in the penultimate step we use the facts that
\bm{B}y\bm{z}=y\bm{B}\bm{z}=\bm{0}
since
\bm{z}\in N(\bm{B})
and
\bm{B}\bm{B}^{{\dagger}}=\bm{I}
since
r\geq m
and
\bm{B}
is full rank by assumption. This gives the first result.

For the second result, we recall that
\widetilde{Y}_{i}
is a closest point to
Y_{i}
from
\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}=\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}})
. Therefore, for any DNN
N
,

\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|Y_{i}-\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ N\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}\geq\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|Y_{i}-\widetilde{Y}_{i}\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}=\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|Y_{i}-\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\widehat{N}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}

as required.
∎

Lemma E.2

(Bounding
\widehat{F}
in terms of

\textstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}

\textstyle F

)
.

Suppose that (
D.7
) holds with
\alpha\geq c_{0}
and
\alpha-\delta\sqrt{k}\geq c_{1}
, and also that

\sqrt{r}{\|\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}-\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{X}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{N}))}\leq c_{2},

where
c_{0},c_{1},c_{2}>0
are suitable universal constants.
Then the approximation
\widehat{F}
satisfies

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\widehat{F}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim

\displaystyle~{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-{\mathchoice{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\displaystyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\textstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.40926pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.40926pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.78334pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=6.14795pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.9537pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.9537pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=3.41667pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=5.20493pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}

\displaystyle+(1+\delta\sqrt{r})\delta\sqrt{k}\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{m}}{\sqrt{r}\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B})}\right)\left({\|F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\sqrt{m}(1+\delta\sqrt{r})}{\sqrt{r}\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B})}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|\widetilde{Y}_{i}-{\mathchoice{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\displaystyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\textstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.40926pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.40926pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.78334pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=6.14795pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.9537pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.9537pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=3.41667pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=5.20493pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}\right)

\displaystyle+(1+\delta\sqrt{r}){\|\bm{z}\|}_{2}

and

\displaystyle{\|F-\widehat{F}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\|F-{\mathchoice{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\displaystyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\textstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.40926pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.40926pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.78334pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=6.14795pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.9537pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.9537pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=3.41667pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=5.20493pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}

\displaystyle+(1+\delta)\sqrt{r}\delta\sqrt{k}\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{m}}{\sqrt{r}\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B})}\right)\left({\|F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\sqrt{m}(1+\delta)}{\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B})}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|\widetilde{Y}_{i}-{\mathchoice{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\displaystyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\textstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.40926pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.40926pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.78334pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=6.14795pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.9537pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.9537pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=3.41667pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=5.20493pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}\right)

\displaystyle+(1+\delta)\sqrt{r}{\|\bm{z}\|}_{2}.

Proof.

By the triangle inequality,

\begin{split}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\widehat{F}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}&\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-{\mathchoice{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\displaystyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\textstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.40926pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.40926pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.78334pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=6.14795pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.9537pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.9537pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=3.41667pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=5.20493pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{\mathchoice{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\displaystyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\textstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.40926pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.40926pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.78334pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=6.14795pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.9537pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.9537pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=3.41667pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=5.20493pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}}-\widehat{F}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\\
{\|F-\widehat{F}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}&\leq{\|F-{\mathchoice{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\displaystyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\textstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.40926pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.40926pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.78334pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=6.14795pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.9537pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.9537pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=3.41667pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=5.20493pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+{\|{\mathchoice{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\displaystyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\textstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.40926pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.40926pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.78334pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=6.14795pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.9537pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.9537pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=3.41667pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=5.20493pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}}-\widehat{F}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\end{split}.

(E.6)

Consider the second term. We have

\begin{split}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{\mathchoice{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\displaystyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\textstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.40926pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.40926pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.78334pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=6.14795pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.9537pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.9537pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=3.41667pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=5.20493pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}}-\widehat{F}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}={|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{p}-\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\widehat{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{p}-\tilde{p}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Y})}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|q|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\\
{\|{\mathchoice{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\displaystyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\textstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.40926pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.40926pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.78334pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=6.14795pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.9537pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.9537pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=3.41667pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=5.20493pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}}-\widehat{F}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}={\|\hat{p}-\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\widehat{N}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\leq{\|\hat{p}-\tilde{p}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Y})}+{\|q\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\end{split},

(E.7)

where
q=\bar{p}-\tilde{p}=\sum^{r}_{i=1}(\bm{B}^{{\dagger}}\bm{e}+y\bm{z})_{i}N_{\bm{e}_{i}}
. Lemma
D.10
and (A.III) give that

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{p}-\tilde{p}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Y})}={\|\hat{p}-\tilde{p}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\leq\delta\sqrt{k}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{p}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}.

(E.8)

Now consider the other term in (
E.7
). Define
\tilde{q}=\sum^{r}_{i=1}(\bm{B}^{{\dagger}}\bm{e}+y\bm{z})_{i}\Psi_{\bm{e}_{i}}
. Then Lemma
D.10
and (A.III) once more give that

\begin{split}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|q|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Y})}&\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\tilde{q}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Y})}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|q-\tilde{q}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\tilde{q}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Y})}+\delta\sqrt{r}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\tilde{q}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\\
{\|q\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Y})}&\leq{\|\tilde{q}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Y})}+{\|q-\tilde{q}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\leq{\|\tilde{q}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Y})}+\delta\sqrt{r}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\tilde{q}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\end{split}.

Here, we also used the fact that
|\Gamma_{o}|=r
. We now apply Lemmas
D.1
and
D.4
and (A.III) once more to get

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|q|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim(1+\delta\sqrt{r}){|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\tilde{q}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})},\qquad{\|q\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\tilde{\varsigma}}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim(1+\delta)\sqrt{r}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\tilde{q}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}.

(E.9)

We next analyze the term
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\tilde{q}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}
. Let
y^{*}\in\mathcal{Y}^{*}
. Since
y^{*}((\bm{B}^{{\dagger}}\bm{e}+y\bm{z})_{i})=(\bm{B}^{{\dagger}}y^{*}(\bm{e})+y^{*}(y)\bm{z})_{i}
, we have

y^{*}(\tilde{q})=\sum^{r}_{i=1}(\bm{B}^{{\dagger}}y^{*}(\bm{e})+y^{*}(y)\bm{z})_{i}\Psi_{\bm{e}_{i}}.

Hence, by Parseval’s identity,

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\tilde{q}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}

\displaystyle=\sup_{y^{*}\in B(\mathcal{Y}^{*})}{\|y^{*}(\tilde{q})\|}_{L^{2}(D)}

\displaystyle=\sup_{y^{*}\in B(\mathcal{Y}^{*})}{\|\bm{B}^{{\dagger}}y^{*}(\bm{e})+y^{*}(y)\bm{z}\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B})}\sup_{y^{*}\in B(\mathcal{Y}^{*})}{\|y^{*}(\bm{e})\|}_{2}+\sup_{y^{*}\in B(\mathcal{Y}^{*})}|y^{*}(y)|{\|\bm{z}\|}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B})}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\bm{e}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}+{\|\bm{z}\|}_{2}.

We now use the definition of
\bm{e}
and the inequality
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\bm{e}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}\leq{\|\bm{e}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}
to obtain

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\tilde{q}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\leq\frac{\sqrt{m}}{\sqrt{r}\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B})}\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|\widetilde{Y}_{i}-\tilde{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}+{\|\bm{z}\|}_{2}.

We next apply the triangle inequality and Lemma
D.10
once more to get

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\tilde{q}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{m}}{\sqrt{r}\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B})}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|\widetilde{Y}_{i}-\hat{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}+{\|\hat{p}-\tilde{p}\|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}\right)+{\|\bm{z}\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{m}}{\sqrt{r}\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B})}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|\widetilde{Y}_{i}-\hat{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}+\delta\sqrt{k}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{p}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}\right)+{\|\bm{z}\|}_{2}.

Combining this with (
E.7
) and (
E.9
), we deduce that

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{\mathchoice{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\displaystyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\textstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.40926pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.40926pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.78334pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=6.14795pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.9537pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.9537pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=3.41667pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=5.20493pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}}-\widehat{F}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim

\displaystyle~(1+\delta\sqrt{r})\Bigg[\delta\sqrt{k}\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{m}}{\sqrt{r}\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B})}\right){|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{p}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}+{\|\bm{z}\|}_{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\sqrt{m}}{\sqrt{r}\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B})}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|\widetilde{Y}_{i}-\hat{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}\right)\Bigg]

and

\displaystyle{\|{\mathchoice{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\displaystyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\textstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.40926pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.40926pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.78334pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=6.14795pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.9537pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.9537pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=3.41667pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=5.20493pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}}-\widehat{F}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim

\displaystyle~(1+\delta)\sqrt{r}\Bigg[\delta\sqrt{k}\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{m}}{\sqrt{r}\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B})}\right){|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{p}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}+{\|\bm{z}\|}_{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\sqrt{m}}{\sqrt{r}\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B})}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|\widetilde{Y}_{i}-\hat{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}\right)\Bigg].

It remains to bound the term
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{p}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}
. Using (
D.7
) and the fact that
\hat{p}
is a minimizer of (
D.3
) and that the zero polynomial is feasible for (
D.3
), we get

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{p}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D;\mathcal{Y})}

\displaystyle\lesssim{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{p}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\tilde{\varsigma}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|Y_{i}-\hat{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{Y}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{Y}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}

\displaystyle\leq 2{\|F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\frac{2}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}

Combining this with the previous bound and (
E.6
) now completes the proof.
∎

E.3
Estimation of
\sigma_{\min}(\textnormal{{B}})

Recall that a matrix
\bm{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times n}
is a
subgaussian random matrix
if its entries are i.i.d. subgaussian random variables with mean zero and variance one (see, e.g.,
[
29
, Def. 9.1]
). The following result can be found in, e.g.,
[
29
, Ex. 9.3]
.

Lemma E.3

(Smallest singular value of a subgaussian random matrix)
.

Let
\bm{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times n}
be a subgaussian random matrix and
\sigma_{\min}
be the smallest singular value of
\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\bm{A}
. Then, for all
0<t<1
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\sigma_{\min}\leq 1-c_{1}\sqrt{n/m}-t\right)\leq 2\exp(-c_{2}mt^{2}),

where
c_{1},c_{2}>0
are universal constants.

Lemma E.4

(Bounding
\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B})
)
.

Suppose that
\sqrt{m}\delta\leq\sqrt{\omega}/8
,

\frac{3^{(5+\xi)/2}\sqrt{r}}{2}{\|\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}-\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{X}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X},\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{N}))}\leq\frac{\sqrt{\omega}}{8},

(E.10)

where
\omega
is the variance of the univariate probability measure as specified in Theorem
3.2
and

d_{\mathcal{X}}\geq r\geq c\left(m+\log(2/\epsilon)\right)

(E.11)

for some universal constant
c\geq 1
.
Then, with probability at least
1-\epsilon
, the matrix
\bm{B}
is full rank and

\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B})\geq\sqrt{\omega}/4.

Proof.

Define the matrices

\bm{B}^{\prime}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{r}}\left(\Psi_{\bm{e}_{j}}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})\right)^{m,r}_{i,j=1},\quad\bm{B}^{\prime\prime}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{r}}\left(\Psi_{\bm{e}_{j}}\circ\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}(X_{i})\right)^{m,r}_{i,j=1}.

Then, since
r\geq m
,

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B})

\displaystyle=\inf\left\{{\|\bm{B}^{\top}\bm{d}\|}_{2}:\bm{d}\in\mathbb{C}^{m},\ {\|\bm{d}\|}_{2}=1\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B}^{\prime})-{\|(\bm{B}-\bm{B}^{\prime})^{\top}\|}_{2}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B}^{\prime})-{\|\bm{B}-\bm{B}^{\prime}\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B}^{\prime\prime})-{\|\bm{B}-\bm{B}^{\prime}\|}_{2}-{\|\bm{B}^{\prime}-\bm{B}^{\prime\prime}\|}_{2}.

Now, for any
\bm{c}\in\mathbb{C}^{r}
,

\displaystyle{\|(\bm{B}-\bm{B}^{\prime})\bm{c}\|}^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r}\sum^{m}_{i=1}\left(\sum^{r}_{j=1}\left(N_{\bm{e}_{j}}(\bm{x}_{i})-\Psi_{\bm{e}_{j}}(\bm{x}_{i})\right)c_{j}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{r}\sum^{m}_{i=1}\delta^{2}{\|\bm{c}\|}^{2}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq m\delta^{2}{\|\bm{c}\|}^{2}_{2}.

We deduce that
{\|\bm{B}-\bm{B}^{\prime}\|}_{2}\leq\sqrt{m}\delta\leq\sqrt{\omega}/8
. Hence

\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B})\geq\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B}^{\prime\prime})-\sqrt{\omega}/8-{\|\bm{B}^{\prime}-\bm{B}^{\prime\prime}\|}_{2}.

Now let
\bm{c}\in\mathbb{C}^{r}
and
p=\sum^{r}_{i=1}c_{i}\Psi_{\bm{e}_{i}}
be the corresponding polynomial. Then, by (A.I), (A.III) and Lemma
D.2
,

\displaystyle{\|(\bm{B^{\prime}}-\bm{B}^{\prime\prime})\bm{c}\|}_{2}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{1}{r}\sum^{m}_{i=1}\left|p\circ\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}(X_{i})-p\circ\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{X}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})\right|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{Lip}(p,B^{\infty}(\mathbb{N}),\mathbb{R}){\|\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}-\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{X}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X},\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{N}))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3^{(5+\xi)/2}\sqrt{r}}{2}{\|p\|}_{L^{2}_{\varrho}(D)}{\|\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}-\iota_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{X}}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X},\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{N}))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{\omega}}{8}{\|\bm{c}\|}_{2}.

Here, in the third step we used the fact that
|\Gamma_{o}|_{\bm{v}}=3^{5+\xi}r
, since
u_{\bm{e}_{i}}=\sqrt{3}
.
We deduce that
{\|\bm{B^{\prime}}-\bm{B}^{\prime\prime}\|}_{2}\leq\sqrt{\omega}/8
and therefore

\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B})\geq\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B}^{\prime\prime})-\sqrt{\omega}/4.

It remains to show that
\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B}^{\prime\prime})\geq\sqrt{\omega}/2
with high probability.
By construction,
\Psi_{\bm{e}_{j}}(\bm{x})=\sqrt{3}x_{j}
. Now recall that the pushforward
\varsigma
is a tensor-product of a univariate probability measure supported in
[-1,1]
with mean zero and variance
\omega>0
. Therefore

(\bm{B}^{\prime\prime})^{\top}=\sqrt{3}\frac{\sqrt{\omega}}{\sqrt{r}}\bm{A},

where
\bm{A}=(\iota(X_{j})_{i}/\sqrt{\omega})^{r,m}_{i,j=1}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times m}
. By construction, the entries of
\bm{A}
are i.i.d. subgaussian random variables with mean zero and variance one. Hence
\bm{A}
is a subgaussian random matrix. We now apply Lemma
E.3
. Let
t=1/4
and observe that

r\geq 4c^{2}_{1}m,\quad r\geq\frac{16}{c_{2}}\log(2/\epsilon),

by assumption. Therefore,

\mathbb{P}(\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B}^{\prime\prime})\leq\sqrt{\omega}/2)\leq\mathbb{P}(\sigma_{\min}(\bm{A}/\sqrt{r})\leq 1/(2\sqrt{3}))\leq\epsilon.

This gives the result.
∎

E.4
Final arguments

We are now ready to complete the proof of Theorem
3.2
.

Proof of Theorem
3.2
, Statement (A).

We divide the proof into a series of steps.

Step 1: Setup.
Let
m
,
\delta
,
\epsilon
and
L
be as in the theorem statement. We once more assume without loss of generality that
\delta\leq 1/5
. Let
n
be as in (
D.29
) and
\Lambda=\Lambda^{\mathsf{HCI}}_{n}
, let
\xi
be as in (
D.30
) and

k=\frac{m}{c_{1}L},

(E.12)

where
c_{1}\geq 1
is a constant that will be chosen in the next step. Let

\delta=\min\left\{2^{-m}/r^{2},\sqrt{\omega}/(8\sqrt{m})\right\},

where
\omega
is the variance of the univariate probability measure and

r=\lceil c_{2}(m+\log(1/\epsilon))\rceil,

(E.13)

where
c_{2}\geq 2
will also be chosen in the next step. Note that
d_{\mathcal{X}}\geq r>m
by assumption. Finally, let
\mathcal{S}
be as in (
D.1
),
\Gamma
be as in (
E.1
) and
\{N_{\bm{\nu}}\}_{\bm{\nu}\in\Gamma}
be the corresponding family of tanh DNNs ensured by Lemma
D.9
. Finally, let
\widehat{F}
be given by (
E.5
), with DNN
\widehat{N}
as in (
E.4
).

Step 2:
\widehat{N}
is a minimizer.
By construction,

\mathrm{width}(\widehat{N})\lesssim(k+r)\cdot m(\Gamma),\quad\mathrm{depth}(\widehat{N})\lesssim\log(k).

If
\bm{\nu}=\bm{e}_{i}
, then
{\|\bm{\nu}\|}_{1}=1
. Hence
m(\Gamma)\leq 1+\max_{S\in\mathcal{S}}m(S)\leq 1+k^{1/(5+\xi)}=1+k^{\delta}
(see §
D.5
). Using the values of
k
and
r
, we see that

\mathrm{width}(\widehat{N})\lesssim(m+\log(1/\epsilon))(m/L)^{\delta},\quad\mathrm{depth}(\widehat{N})\lesssim\log(m/L).

Therefore,
\widehat{N}\in\mathcal{N}
is feasible for (
2.5
). Lemma
E.1
now implies that
\widehat{N}
is a minimizer, provided
\bm{B}
is full rank. We will show that this holds in the next step.

Step 3: Ensuring that
\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B})\geq\sqrt{\omega}/4
with probability at least
1-\epsilon/4
.
We seek to use Lemma
E.4
. By definition of
\delta
, we have that
\sqrt{m}\delta\leq\sqrt{\omega}/8
. Now (
D.34
), (
E.13
) and (
3.9
) imply that (
E.10
)-(
E.11
) hold, the latter with
\epsilon
replaced by
\epsilon/4
. Hence Lemma
E.4
implies the result.

Step 4: Ensuring (
D.7
) holds with probability at least
1-\epsilon/4
.
This step is very similar to Step 2 of the proof of Theorem
3.1
. The only difference comes in the estimation of
N
in (
D.32
), since now
N=|\Gamma|\leq|\Lambda|+r
. Since
m\geq 3
, we have

\log(\mathrm{e}N)\leq\log(\mathrm{e}|\Lambda|(r+1))\lesssim\log^{2}(m)+\log(m+\log(1/\epsilon)).

If
m\leq\log(1/\epsilon)
then

\log(\mathrm{e}N)\lesssim\log^{2}(m)+\log(\log(1/\epsilon))\leq\log^{2}(m)+\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon)},

where in the second step we use the fact that
\log(t)\leq\sqrt{t}
for
t>0
. Conversely, if
m\geq\log(1/\epsilon)
, then

\log(\mathrm{e}N)\lesssim\log^{2}(m)\leq\log^{2}(m)+\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon)}.

Therefore, (
D.33
) with
\epsilon/4
reads

\displaystyle c_{0}\cdot k\cdot(\log(\mathrm{e}N)\cdot\log^{2}(k)+\log(6/\epsilon))

\displaystyle\lesssim k\cdot\left(\log^{2}(m)\left(\log^{2}(m)+\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon)}\right)+\log(1/\epsilon)\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim k\cdot\left(\log^{4}(m)+\log(1/\epsilon)\right).

\displaystyle=k\cdot L(m,\epsilon).

Hence, due to the definition of
k
, we get that (
D.13
) holds once more with probability at least
1-\epsilon/4
. The remainder of this step is identical to Step 2 of the proof of Theorem
3.1
.

Step 5: Error analysis.
We now apply Lemma
E.2
to the approximation
\widehat{F}
. Since
\sigma_{\min}(\bm{B})\gtrsim\sqrt{\omega}\gtrsim 1
,
\delta\leq\delta\sqrt{r}\leq 1
and
r\geq m
, we deduce that

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\widehat{F}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim

\displaystyle~{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-{\mathchoice{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\displaystyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\textstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.40926pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.40926pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.78334pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=6.14795pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.9537pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.9537pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=3.41667pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=5.20493pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\delta\sqrt{k}\left({\|F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}\right)

\displaystyle+{\|\bm{z}\|}_{2}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|\widetilde{Y}_{i}-{\mathchoice{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\displaystyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\textstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.40926pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.40926pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.78334pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=6.14795pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.9537pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.9537pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=3.41667pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=5.20493pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}

and

\displaystyle{\|F-\widehat{F}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\|F-{\mathchoice{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\displaystyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\textstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.40926pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.40926pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.78334pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=6.14795pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.9537pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.9537pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=3.41667pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=5.20493pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{r}\delta\sqrt{k}\left({\|F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}\right)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{r}{\|\bm{z}\|}_{2}+\sqrt{m}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|\widetilde{Y}_{i}-{\mathchoice{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\displaystyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\textstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.40926pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.40926pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.78334pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=6.14795pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.9537pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.9537pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=3.41667pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=5.20493pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}\right)

with probability at least
1-\epsilon/2
, where
{\mathchoice{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\displaystyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\textstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.40926pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.40926pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.78334pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=6.14795pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.9537pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.9537pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=3.41667pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=5.20493pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}}=\hat{p}\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}
and
\hat{p}
is the corresponding minimizer of (
D.3
). We now appeal to Theorem
D.6
with
\sigma=1
and
\tau=0
, recalling that
\alpha\gtrsim 1
and
\delta\sqrt{k}\lesssim 1
, to get that

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\widehat{F}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim

\displaystyle~{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}

\displaystyle+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}\left({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}+\delta\sqrt{k}{\|F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}

\displaystyle+{\|\bm{z}\|}_{2}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|\widetilde{Y}_{i}-{\mathchoice{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\displaystyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\textstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.40926pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.40926pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.78334pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=6.14795pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.9537pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.9537pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=3.41667pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=5.20493pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}

and, since
\delta\sqrt{r}\lesssim 1
,

\displaystyle{\|F-\widehat{F}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{k}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}

\displaystyle+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}\left({\|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{k}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}\right)

\displaystyle+{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}+\sqrt{r}\delta\sqrt{k}{\|F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{r}{\|\bm{z}\|}_{2}+\sqrt{m}\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|\widetilde{Y}_{i}-{\mathchoice{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\displaystyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\textstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.40926pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.40926pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.78334pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=6.14795pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.9537pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.9537pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=3.41667pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=5.20493pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}

for any
q\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S};\mathcal{Y}}
and linear operator
\mathcal{Q}:\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}=\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}})
with
\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}={\|\mathcal{Q}\|}_{\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}}
. Consider the final term. Since
\widetilde{Y}_{i}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}
is the closest point to
Y_{i}=F(X_{i})+E_{i}
we have

{\|\widetilde{Y}_{i}-Y_{i}\|}_{\mathcal{Y}}\leq{\|F(X_{i})-\mathcal{Q}\circ F(X_{i})\|}_{\mathcal{Y}}+{\|E_{i}\|}_{\mathcal{Y}}.

We now use this, the fact that
\hat{p}
is a minimizer and
\mathcal{Q}\circ q
is feasible in combination with triangle inequality to get

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|\widetilde{Y}_{i}-{\mathchoice{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\displaystyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 7.09259pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 7.09259pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=6.83331pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=7.81946pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\textstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.40926pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.40926pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.78334pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=6.14795pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.9537pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.9537pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=3.41667pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=5.20493pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}\crcr}}}}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{i=1}{\|Y_{i}-\mathcal{Q}\circ q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathcal{Y}}}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}

\displaystyle\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}+\frac{2}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}

\displaystyle\leq\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-q\circ\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}+2{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}+\frac{2}{\sqrt{m}}{\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}.

Now let
f\in\mathcal{H}(\bm{b})
be the function asserted by (A.II) and
q=f_{S}
be the polynomial asserted by Lemma
D.17
.
Substituting this into the previous expression, recalling (
D.35
) and using the definition of
\delta
, we deduce that

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\widehat{F}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim

\displaystyle~{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}

\displaystyle+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}E_{2}(F,q)+{\|\bm{z}\|}_{2}+2^{-m}+E_{\mathsf{samp},2}

\displaystyle{\|F-\widehat{F}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{m}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\mathcal{Q}\circ F|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathsf{disc},\mu}

\displaystyle+\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}\widetilde{E}_{\infty}(F,q)+\sqrt{r}{\|\bm{z}\|}_{2}+2^{-m}+E^{\prime}_{\mathsf{samp},\infty}

with probability at least
1-\epsilon/2
. Here
E_{2}(F,q)
is as in (
D.24
),
\widetilde{E}_{\infty}(F,q)
is as in (
D.24
) with
k
replaced by
m
and
E^{\prime}_{\mathsf{samp},\infty}={\|\bm{E}\|}_{2;\mathcal{Y}}=\sqrt{L}E_{\mathsf{samp},\infty}
.

Now observe that the first bound is identical to the corresponding bound in
(
D.36
), except with
E_{\mathsf{opt},2}
replaced by
{\|\bm{z}\|}_{2}+2^{-m}
. Following the same arguments as in Step 3 of the proof of Theorem
3.1
, this gives the corresponding bound in (
D.37
), which is

\begin{split}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\widehat{F}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim&~a_{\mathcal{Y}}\left({\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}/\sqrt{m/L}+{\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\right)\\
&+E_{\mathcal{X},2}+E_{\mathcal{Y},2}+{\|\bm{z}\|}_{2}+2^{-m}+E_{\mathsf{samp},2}.\end{split}

(E.14)

The second bound above is identical to the corresponding bound in
(
D.36
), except with
E_{\mathsf{opt},\infty}
replaced by
\sqrt{r}{\|\bm{z}\|}_{2}+2^{-m}
,
E_{\mathsf{samp},\infty}
and
E_{\infty}(F,q)
replaced by
E^{\prime}_{\mathsf{samp},\infty}
and
\widetilde{E}_{\infty}(F,q)
, respectively, and with
\sqrt{k}
replaced by
\sqrt{m}
. We once more follow the same arguments as in Step 3, with these changes. This yields the corresponding version of (
D.37
), which is

\begin{split}{\|F-\widehat{F}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim&~a_{\mathcal{Y}}\left(\sqrt{L}{\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}+\sqrt{m}{\|F-q\circ\iota\|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\right)\\
&+E^{\prime}_{\mathcal{X},\infty}+E^{\prime}_{\mathcal{Y},\infty}+\sqrt{r}{\|\bm{z}\|}_{2}+2^{-m}+E^{\prime}_{\mathsf{samp},\infty}.\end{split}

(E.15)

Here
E^{\prime}_{\mathcal{X},\infty}=LE_{\mathcal{X},\infty}
and
E^{\prime}_{\mathcal{Y},\infty}=\sqrt{L}E_{\mathcal{Y},\infty}
.

Having done this, we then use the bounds from Step 4 of the proof of Theorem
3.1
, to get

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|F-\widehat{F}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{L^{2}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim

\displaystyle~E_{\mathsf{app},2}+E_{\mathcal{X},2}+E_{\mathcal{Y},2}+{\|\bm{z}\|}_{2}+2^{-m}+E_{\mathsf{samp},2}

\displaystyle{\|F-\widehat{F}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\mu}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Y})}\lesssim

\displaystyle~E^{\prime}_{\mathsf{app},\infty}+E^{\prime}_{\mathcal{X},\infty}+E^{\prime}_{\mathcal{Y},\infty}+\sqrt{r}{\|\bm{z}\|}_{2}+2^{-m}+E^{\prime}_{\mathsf{samp},\infty},

where
E^{\prime}_{\mathsf{app},\infty}=\sqrt{L}E_{\mathsf{app},\infty}
.

Step 6: Existence of uncountably many minimizers.
Let
\bm{z}=(z_{i})^{r}_{i=1}\in N(\bm{B})\backslash\{\bm{0}\}
be any vector and consider
\bm{z}_{1}=\theta_{1}\bm{z}
and
\bm{z}_{2}=\theta_{2}\bm{z}
for
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in[-1,1]
with
\theta_{1}\neq\theta_{2}
.
Then these vectors define functions
\bar{p}_{1}
and
\bar{p}_{2}
as in (
E.2
) and DNNs
\widehat{N}_{1}
and
\widehat{N}_{2}
as in (
E.3
). Suppose that
\widehat{N}_{1}=\widehat{N}_{2}
. Then, since
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}
is linear, we have that
\bar{p}_{1}=\bar{p}_{2}
